> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (p. 121):
> Thus it appears in al-Fatāwā, but this is a textual error; the correct reading is “two hundred years.” The ʿUbaydids entered Egypt in 358 and remained there until Asad al-Dīn Shīrkūh and his nephew Salāh al-Dīn al-Ayyūbī entered it in 564 and restored the Friday sermon in the Abbasid caliph’s name. Al-ʿĀdid, the last of the ʿUbaydids, died in 567. Their total duration in Egypt was therefore slightly more than two hundred years. The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—mentioned the ʿUbaydids and their duration in Egypt in several places, including his statement in al-Minhāj, 6:342: “The period of rule of the Banū ʿUbayd Allāh al-Qaddāh, who remained in the Maghrib for a time and in Egypt for about two hundred years. These were heretics, by the unanimous agreement of scholars and the devout.”

---

## Page 6818

Thus, faith in the Messenger and jihad in defence of his religion were a cause of good in this world and the Hereafter. Conversely, religious innovations, heresy, and opposition to what he brought are a cause of evil in this world and the Hereafter. When heresy and religious innovations spread in Syria, Egypt, and al-Jazīra, the unbelievers were given power over their people. But when they upheld Islam to the extent that they did and subdued the heretics and religious innovators, Allah granted them victory over the unbelievers, in fulfilment of His words:

“O you who believe, shall I direct you to a transaction that will save you from a painful punishment? Believe in Allah and His Messenger and strive in Allah’s cause with your wealth and your lives. That is better for you, if only you knew. He will forgive your sins and admit you into gardens beneath which rivers flow, and pleasant dwellings in Gardens of perpetual residence. That is the great triumph. And He will grant you something else that you love: help from Allah and an imminent victory. So give glad tidings to the believers.”

Likewise, when the people of the East upheld Islam, they were granted victory over the polytheistic unbelievers among the Turks, the Indians, the Chinese, and others. But when religious innovations, heresy, and wickedness spread among them as they did, the unbelievers were given power over them. The Most High said:

“We decreed for the Children of Israel in the Scripture: ‘You will surely spread corruption in the land twice, and you will surely become exceedingly arrogant.’ When the first promise came to pass, We sent against you servants of Ours endowed with great military might, and they swept through your homes. It was a promise fulfilled. Then We restored your ascendancy over them, supplied you with wealth and sons, and made you greater in numbers. If you do good, you do good for yourselves; and if you do evil, it is against yourselves. Then, when the latter promise came to pass

---

## Page 6819

, your enemies were sent to bring grief to your faces, to enter the sanctuary as they had entered it the first time, and to destroy utterly whatever they conquered. Perhaps your Lord will have mercy on you; but if you return, We shall return. And We have made Hell a prison for the unbelievers.”

Some shaykhs used to say that Hulagu—the ruler of the Tatar Turks who overpowered the caliph in Iraq and carried out an immense massacre in Baghdad, reportedly killing a million people, and likewise perpetrated killings in Aleppo, then the royal capital—was, as some shaykhs put it, to the Muslims what Nebuchadnezzar had been to the Children of Israel.

Among the reasons these people invaded Muslim lands was the spread of heresy, hypocrisy, and religious innovations. Matters reached the point that al-Rāzī composed a book on worshipping celestial bodies and idols and practising magic, which he entitled al-Sirr al-maktūm fī al-sihr wa-mukhātabat al-nujūm (The Hidden Secret of Magic and Addressing the Stars). It is said that he composed it for the mother of Sultan ‘Alā’ al-Dīn Muhammad ibn Laksh ibn Jalāl al-Dīn Khwārazm Shāh, who was among the mightiest kings on earth. Al-Rāzī had such close ties with him that he entrusted him with the guardianship of his children. He also composed a book for him entitled al-Risāla al-‘Alā’iyya fī al-ikhtiyārāt al-samāwiyya (The Treatise for ‘Alā’ al-Dīn on Astrological Choices).

For the misguided, these astrological choices replace seeking Allah’s guidance, which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taught the Muslims. As Jābir said in the authentic hadith related by al-Bukhārī and others: “The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to teach us

---

## Page 6820

to seek Allah’s guidance in all matters, just as he taught us a surah of the Quran. He would say:

‘When any of you intends to undertake something, let him pray two units of prayer apart from the obligatory prayer, then say: “O Allah, I seek Your guidance through Your knowledge, and I seek strength through Your power, and I ask You of Your immense bounty. O Allah, if You know that this matter—naming it specifically—is good for me in my religion, my livelihood, and the ultimate outcome of my affairs, then decree it for me, make it easy, and bless it for me. But if You know that this matter is bad for me in my religion, my livelihood, and the ultimate outcome of my affairs, then turn it away from me and turn me away from it. Decree what is good for me wherever it may be, then make me content with it.”’”

Astrologers, however, have their own choices of auspicious times. When one of them wishes to undertake an action, he selects an auspicious time according to the rising stars and performs that action then, so that it will succeed—or so they claim. Scholars have written books refuting them, describing how frequently their predictions and prescriptions produce results contrary to what they intend. How many of their predictions prove false, and how many of the times they recommend turn out badly!

Al-Rāzī composed his Astrological Choices for this king, including in it the selection of auspicious times for drinking wine and other activities. Likewise, in al-Sirr al-maktūm (The Hidden Secret), he discussed worshipping and invoking the celestial bodies, prostrating to them, associating them with Allah in worship, and calling upon them as those who worship Allah alone call upon their Lord—indeed, with even greater fervour. He also discussed seeking nearness to them through acts of disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience thought to accord with their nature. Thus, he stated that nearness to Venus is sought through committing indecencies, drinking wine, singing, and similar things that Allah and His Messenger have forbidden.

---

## Page 6821

In reality, this brings them closer to the devils who command them to do such things and tell them, “The celestial body itself loves this.” In fact, the celestial bodies are subject to Allah’s command and obedient to Him; they command neither associating partners with Him nor any other sin. Rather, it is the devils who command such things. They call these devils the spirits of the celestial bodies and may even regard them as angels, whereas they are nothing but devils.

When such heresy and religious innovations spread in the eastern lands through rulers like this king and scholars like this scholar, Allah unleashed against them the disbelieving, polytheistic Turks, who destroyed this kingdom. Events befell it that hold a lesson for anyone who takes heed and recognizes the fulfilment of what Allah has proclaimed in His Book, where He says:

“We will show them Our signs on the horizons and within themselves until it becomes clear to them that it is the truth.”

That is, that the Quran is true. He also says:

“I will show you My signs, so do not ask Me to hasten.”

A fuller discussion of this belongs elsewhere.

“The point here” is that the Umayyad dynasty came to an end because of this al-Ja'd, who denied the divine attributes, and other causes that brought about its decline. Towards the end of their rule, al-Jahm ibn Safwān emerged in Khurāsān—it has been said that he was originally from Tirmidh—and openly proclaimed the doctrine of the Jahmiyyah, who deny and negate the divine attributes. He was killed in one of the battles.

The leading Muslim scholars in the East knew the true nature of his doctrine better than the scholars of the Hijaz, Syria, and Iraq. Hence, more statements concerning the Jahmiyyah are found from 'Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak and other Muslim scholars in the East than from scholars elsewhere, even though most of the

---

## Page 6822

leading Muslim scholars spoke about them. Yet they were prominent only in the East. Their influence grew, however, when al-Rashīd died and his son, known as al-Ma'mūn, assumed authority in the East and absorbed their teachings. Then, when he became caliph, he met with many of them and, towards the end of his life, called people to adopt their doctrine.

He wrote to Baghdad while at the frontier stronghold of Tarsus, in the land of Sis. At that time, it was Baghdad’s foremost frontier stronghold and one of the greatest frontier strongholds of the Muslims. Devout people came there from every region to serve on frontier guard duty. Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal—may Allah be pleased with him—al-Sarī al-Saqatī, and others served there. Abū 'Ubayd held the office of judge there, as did Sālih ibn Ahmad ibn Hanbal. It is therefore frequently mentioned in books of jurisprudence, for it was a major frontier stronghold.

From that frontier stronghold, he wrote to his deputy in Baghdad, Ishāq ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Mus'ab, calling upon people to declare, “The Quran is created.” No one complied. He then wrote a second letter, ordering that anyone who refused to comply be put in chains and sent to him. Most of them then complied. Seven who had refused were put in chains; five of these complied after being chained. Two remained who would not comply: Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal and Muhammad ibn Nūh. They sent the two to him, but he died before they reached him. He had designated his brother Abū Ishāq as his successor. This was in 218 AH.

Ahmad ibn Hanbal remained imprisoned until 220 AH. The debates then unfolded as they did, until he silenced them with his arguments. Then, fearing unrest, they flogged him and released him, and the doctrine of

---

## Page 6823

the Jahmīs, who denied Allah’s attributes, became prevalent. They put people to the test: those who complied received stipends, while those who refused were denied stipends, dismissed from public office, and had their testimony rejected. When ransoming captives, they would test each captive: if he complied, they ransomed him; otherwise, they did not. Their judge, Ahmad ibn Abī Du’ād, inscribed on the covering of the Kaaba:

“There is nothing like Him, and He is the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

He did not write, “and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.”

Then al-Wāthiq assumed power, and matters grew worse until al-Mutawakkil became caliph and ended the inquisition. The Sunnah then prevailed. A detailed account of this belongs elsewhere.

The point is that when the leading Muslim scholars recognized the true nature of the Jahmīs’ doctrine, they explained it clearly. Indeed, ‘Abd Allāh ibn al-Mubārak said: “We can recount the statements of the Jews and Christians, but we cannot bring ourselves to recount the statements of the Jahmīs.” He would recite:

I marvel at a devil who openly called people to the Fire,
And whose name was derived from Jahannam (Hell).

He was asked, “How do we know our Lord?”

He replied, “By knowing that He is above His heavens, upon His Throne, separate from His creation.”

He was asked, “With a limit?”

He replied, “With a limit.”

Ahmad ibn Hanbal, Ishāq ibn Ibrāhīm ibn Rāhawayh, ‘Uthmān ibn Sa‘īd al-Dārimī, and other leading scholars of the Sunnah likewise affirmed this.

---

## Page 6824

The true substance of the doctrine of the Jahmīs who deny Allah’s attributes is Pharaoh’s doctrine: denying the Creator and nullifying His speech and His religion. This is what Pharaoh did: he denied the Creator—majestic is His glory—and said:

“I know of no god for you other than myself.”

He said to Moses:

“If you take a god other than me, I will surely have you imprisoned.”

And he said:

“I am your lord most high.”

He denied that Allah had spoken to Moses or that Moses had a God above the heavens. He wanted to abolish worship of Allah and obedience to Him, and to be the one worshipped and obeyed himself.

Since the doctrine of the Jahmīs who negate and deny Allah’s attributes leads to Pharaoh’s doctrine, its ultimate outcome was denial of the Lord of all worlds, denial of worshipping Him, and denial of His speech. Eventually, they came forward claiming spiritual realization, monotheism, and gnosis, and began to say:

“The world is Allah, and existence is one. The beginningless, eternal Creator is the very being that came into existence and was created. The Lord is the servant; there is no distinction between Lord and servant, or Creator and creation.”

Rather, they regard such distinctions as separation. Thus they began finding fault with the prophets and disparaging them. They found fault with Noah, Abraham—the close friend of Allah—and others, while praising Pharaoh and permitting the worship of all created beings and all idols. Nor were they content merely with idols being worshipped; they went so far as to say:

“Idolaters have worshipped none but Allah. Allah Himself is both the worshipper and the worshipped, and He is the entirety of existence.”

Thus they denied the Lord and nullified His religion, His commands, and His prohibitions

---

## Page 6825

and the message with which He sent His messengers, and His speaking to Moses and others.

A number of people versed in speculative theology, philosophy, and the forms of Sufism associated with them have gone astray in this matter, including Ibn Sabʿīn, al-Sadr al-Qūnawī—the disciple of Ibn ʿArabī—al-Balyānī, and al-Tilimsānī. The latter was among their most accomplished in learning and understanding. He openly put the doctrine into practice, drinking wine and committing forbidden acts.

A trustworthy man told me that he had studied Ibn ʿArabī’s Fusūs al-Hikam (The Bezels of Wisdom) under him, believing it to contain the words of Allah’s friends who possessed knowledge of Him. But when he read it, he found that it contradicted the Quran. He said: “I told him, ‘These words contradict the Quran.’ He replied, ‘The Quran is all polytheism; monotheism is found only in our words.’” He also used to say: “Through unveiling, we have established things that contradict the plain dictates of reason.”

Someone who had accompanied him and another man like him told me that the two passed a dead, mangy dog on the road near Dār al-Tuʿm. His companion asked him: “Is this, too, the very essence of Allah?” He replied: “Is there anything outside it? Yes, everything is within His essence.”

The true substance of these people’s doctrine is Pharaoh’s doctrine. But Pharaoh feared no one such that he would need to conceal his beliefs from him, so he did not acknowledge the Creator, although inwardly he affirmed Him and knew that he himself was nothing but a created being. But his love of supremacy in the land and of wrongdoing

---

## Page 6826

drove him to denial and rejection, as He said:

“But when Our signs came to them, clear to see, they said, ‘This is obvious magic.’”

“And they denied them, though their souls were convinced of them, out of injustice and arrogance. So see what was the end of those who spread corruption.”

As for these people, in one respect they act hypocritically towards the Muslims, so they cannot openly deny the Creator. In another respect, they are misguided people who imagine that they follow the truth and that the Creator is the created. Their doctrine is thus Pharaoh’s doctrine. Pharaoh, however, was obstinate, openly displaying his denial and defiance, whereas these people are either ignorant and misguided or hypocrites who conceal heresy and denial while outwardly agreeing with the Muslims.

Shaykh ʿAbd al-Sayyid, who had been a judge among the Jews and then embraced Islam, told me the following. He was among the most truthful of people and among the finest Muslims, exemplary in his adherence to Islam. He used to meet with one of their shaykhs, known as al-Sharaf al-Balāsī, who was sought out for knowledge and learning.

He said: “He invited me to embrace this doctrine, so I told him, ‘Your doctrine resembles Pharaoh’s doctrine.’ He replied, ‘We do indeed follow Pharaoh’s doctrine.’”

I asked ʿAbd al-Sayyid, “Did he admit this to you?”

He replied, “Yes.”

At that time, ʿAbd al-Sayyid had discussed this doctrine with me, and I had told him: “This is a corrupt doctrine, and it ultimately amounts to Pharaoh’s doctrine.” He then related this incident to me, and I told him: “I did not think they would admit that they followed Pharaoh’s doctrine. But when an opponent admits his position, no evidence is needed.”

ʿAbd al-Sayyid said: “Then I told him, ‘I will not abandon Moses and go

---

## Page 6827

to Pharaoh.’ He asked, ‘Why?’ I replied, ‘Because Moses drowned Pharaoh.’ He had no answer.”

He had argued against him on the basis of worldly ascendancy. I said to ʿAbd al-Sayyid—this had taken place before he embraced Islam—“Your Judaism served you well. A Jew is better than a follower of Pharaoh.”

Among them are groups devoted to worship and asceticism, sincere in what they practise and believing it to be true. Most of them—those who acknowledge both outwardly and inwardly that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah and the best of creation, superior to all prophets and saints—do not understand the true import of their doctrine. Rather, they suppose that it is the realization of what the Messenger brought, and that it resembles the words of those endowed with spiritual knowledge who speak about the realities of faith and religion and are among Allah’s most distinguished saints. They therefore imagine that these people are like al-Fudayl ibn ʿIyād, Ibrāhīm ibn Adham, Abū Sulaymān al-Dārānī, al-Sarī al-Saqatī, al-Junayd ibn Muhammad, Sahl ibn ʿAbd Allāh, and others like them.

Their adepts, however, who understand the true import of their doctrine, know that this is not the case. They say what Ibn ʿArabī and others like him say: that the saints are superior to the prophets; that the Seal of the Saints is superior to the Seal of the Prophets; that all prophets derive their knowledge of Allah from the lamp-niche of the Seal of the Saints; and that he draws from the same source as the angel who comes to the Seal of the Prophets. They have adopted Jahmī doctrines and philosophical speculation, presenting the teachings of the philosophers and the Jahmiyyah in the guise of spiritual unveiling.

---

## Page 6828

And according to the philosophers, Gabriel is merely an image in the Prophet’s mind, not an angel who comes from heaven. In their view, the Prophet receives from this image, whereas the Seal of the Saints, they claim, receives from the immaterial intellect from which the image itself receives. Thus, he draws from the same source as the angel who conveys revelation to the Messenger.

They exalt Pharaoh and repeat the words of the author of al-Fusūs (The Bezels), who said:

“Since Pharaoh occupied the position of command as the master of his age, and this accorded with the conventions of religious law, he therefore said, ‘I am your lord, most high.’ That is: ‘Although all are lords in some respect, I am the highest among them by virtue of the outward authority over you that I have been given.’”

He said: “When the sorcerers knew that Pharaoh spoke truthfully, they did not reject his statement but acknowledged it, saying to him, ‘So decree whatever you will decree. Your decree concerns only this worldly life.’”

He said: “Thus Pharaoh’s statement, ‘I am your lord, most high,’ was true, even though Pharaoh was identical with the Real.”

A trustworthy man who had belonged to them but later renounced their ways told me that the person they hated most was Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

He said: “When a donkey brays or a dog barks, they prostrate to it and say, ‘This is Allah, for it is one of the manifestations.’”

He said: “I said to him, ‘Muhammad ibn Abd Allāh is also one of the manifestations, so treat him like the other manifestations, since you venerate them all—or else say nothing about him.’”

He said: “They replied to me, ‘Muhammad—we hate him because he proclaimed the distinction, called people to it, and punished those who did not affirm it.’”

He said:

---

## Page 6829

“Thus they contradicted themselves in their false doctrine and deemed the dog and the donkey superior to the best of creation.” He told me: “They openly curse him and other prophets.”

There is no doubt that they are among the foremost worshippers of Satan and disbelievers in the Most Merciful. It is established in the authentic hadith collection that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When you hear roosters crowing, ask Allah of His bounty, for they have seen an angel. When you hear a donkey braying or a dog barking, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, for they have seen a devil.”

Thus, when they hear a donkey braying or a dog barking, devils are present, and their prostration is therefore to the devils.

Among them was a distinguished shaykh, one of their most accomplished—but he was not among those who reviled the prophets. He wrote a book entitled Fakk al-Azrār ʿan Aʿnāq al-Asrār (Unfastening the Buttons from the Necks of Mysteries), in which he recounted a conversation he had with Iblīs. Iblīs said to him, in substance:

“You have overcome me and overpowered me,” and words to that effect, “but something happened to me with one of your shaykhs that astonished me. I manifested myself to him and said, ‘I am Allah; there is no god but Me.’ He prostrated to me, and I was astonished that he should do so.”

This shaykh said:

“I told him, ‘That man is the best and most knowledgeable among us. You did not understand his intention. He did not see two in existence; he saw only one, and so he prostrated to that one, making no distinction between Iblīs and anyone else.’”

Thus this shaykh regarded that man who prostrated to Iblīs, making no distinction

---

## Page 6830

between the Lord and anyone else—indeed, who regarded Iblīs and all other existing beings as Allah—to be the best and most knowledgeable among them.

For this reason, Ibn ʿArabī disparaged Noah, the first messenger sent to the people of the earth. Allah made his descendants the sole survivors, saved him and those with him in the Ark, and destroyed all the other inhabitants of the earth when they rejected him. He remained among his people for a thousand years less fifty. Yet Ibn ʿArabī exalted Noah’s disbelieving people, who worshipped idols, claiming that they had worshipped none but Allah and that their sins had carried them onward, so that they drowned in seas of knowledge of Allah. This is his habitual practice: he disparages the prophets and praises the disbelievers, as he did in his accounts of Noah, Abraham, Moses, Aaron, and others. He praised the worshippers of the calf, disparaged Aaron, and falsely attributed statements to Moses.

He said:

“Moses understood the matter better than Aaron, for he knew what the worshippers of the calf had worshipped, knowing that Allah had decreed that none but He should be worshipped, and that whatever Allah decrees must occur. Thus, when this happened, Moses reproached his brother Aaron for condemning it and being unable to accommodate it. For the gnostic is one who sees the Real in everything—indeed, sees Him as identical with everything.”

He thus claimed that Moses reproached Aaron for condemning their worship of the calf, and that Aaron condemned it because he could not accommodate it; for the gnostic is one who sees the Real in everything—indeed, sees Him as identical with everything.

---

## Page 6831

This is among the gravest fabrications against Moses and Aaron, against Allah, and against the worshippers of the calf. Allah tells us that Moses condemned the calf more vehemently than Aaron did, and that he seized Aaron by his beard because he had not left them and followed Moses to inform him. Allah, the Exalted, says:

> “And what made you hasten ahead of your people, O Moses?”
>
> He said, “They are close behind me, and I hastened to You, my Lord, so that You might be pleased.”
>
> He said, “We have indeed tested your people after your departure, and al-Sāmirī has led them astray.”
>
> So Moses returned to his people, angry and grieved. He said, “O my people, did your Lord not make you a good promise? Did the time seem too long to you, or did you wish that wrath from your Lord should descend upon you, so you broke your promise to me?”
>
> They said, “We did not break our promise to you of our own accord, but we were burdened with loads of the people’s jewellery, which we cast away; and al-Sāmirī likewise cast something in.”
>
> Then he produced for them a calf—a body that made a lowing sound—and they said, “This is your god and the god of Moses, but he forgot.”
>
> Did they not see that it could not answer them, nor had it any power to harm or benefit them?
>
> Aaron had already told them, “O my people, you are only being tested by it. Your Lord is indeed the Most Merciful, so follow me and obey my command.”
>
> They said, “We will not cease devoting ourselves to it until Moses returns to us.”
>
> Moses said, “O Aaron, what prevented you, when you saw them going astray, from following me? Did you then disobey my command?”
>
> He said, “O son of my mother, do not seize me by my beard or my head. I feared that you would say, ‘You have caused division among the Children of Israel and have not heeded my words.’”

I said to one of these people, “This statement that this man has related about Moses

---

## Page 6832

and Aaron—does it agree with the Quran or contradict it?”

He replied, “No; rather, it contradicts it.” I said, “Then choose for yourself: either the Quran or the words of Ibn Arabī.”

Likewise, he said concerning Noah: “Had Noah combined the two calls for his people, they would have responded to him.” That is, he mentioned his calling them openly and then calling them secretly. He went on to say:

“When they realized that calling to Allah was a ruse played upon those being called—for He was not absent at the beginning, such that they should be called to Him as their destination—‘I call to Allah’ is the very essence of the ruse. ‘With insight’—he thus indicated that the matter belongs entirely to Allah. So they answered him with a ruse, just as he had called them.

“Then the Muhammadan came and knew that the call to Allah concerns not His identity, but His names. Thus He said: ‘On the Day We gather the righteous before the Most Merciful as an honoured delegation.’ He used the preposition indicating a destination and coupled it with the Name. We therefore knew that creation was encompassed by a divine name that required them to be God-fearing.

“So they said as part of their ruse: ‘Do not abandon your gods; do not abandon Wadd, Suwā', Yaghūth, Ya'ūq, or Nasr.’ For, to the extent that they abandoned these, they would be ignorant of the Real. The Real has an aspect in every object of worship, recognized by those who recognize it and unknown to those who do not. As He said concerning the Muhammadans: ‘Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him, and that you show kindness to parents’—that is, He has ruled.

“The knower knows whom he has worshipped and in what form He appeared so as to be worshipped. He knows that differentiation and multiplicity are like the limbs within a perceptible form and the immaterial faculties within a spiritual form. Thus, in every object of worship, none other than Allah has been worshipped.”

---

## Page 6833

He continually distorts the Quran, taking its words out of their proper context, as when he said concerning this account:

“‘Because of their sins’—it was these that carried them onward, so they drowned in the seas of knowledge of Allah, which is bewilderment. ‘And were cast into a Fire’—within water itself. Concerning the Muhammadans: ‘And when the seas are set ablaze.’ ‘I stoked the oven’ means ‘I lit it.’ ‘And they found no helpers besides Allah’—so Allah was the very essence of their helpers, and they perished in Him forever.”

Allah’s words, “Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him,” mean that He commanded, obligated and ordained this. In another reading: “Your Lord has enjoined that you worship none but Him.” Yet he construed this to mean that Allah decreed and willed that you worship none but Him—and whatever He decrees comes to pass. He thus made it mean that every object of worship is Allah, and that no one has ever worshipped anything other than Allah. This is among the most blatant fabrications against Allah, His Book, His religion and the people of the earth. For Allah has stated in numerous places that the polytheists worshipped others besides Allah. Indeed, they worship Satan, as the Exalted said:

“Did I not enjoin upon you, O children of Adam, that you should not worship Satan—for he is your clear enemy—and that you should worship Me? This is a straight path. He has led great multitudes of you astray; did you not use your reason?”

The Exalted also related that Joseph said:

“O my two fellow prisoners, are many different lords better, or Allah, the One, the All-Subduing? You worship besides Him nothing but names that you and your forefathers have devised, for which Allah has sent down no authority. Judgement belongs to Allah alone. He has commanded that you worship none but Him. That is the upright religion, but most people do not know.”

And the Exalted said:

---

## Page 6834

“We brought the Children of Israel across the sea, and they came upon a people devoted to their idols. They said, ‘O Moses, make us a god just as they have gods.’ He said, ‘You are indeed an ignorant people.’”

“‘What these people follow is doomed to destruction, and all they have been doing is false.’”

“He said, ‘Should I seek a god for you other than Allah, when He has favoured you above all peoples?’”

The Exalted said concerning Abraham, His close friend:

“When he said to his father, ‘O my father, why do you worship what can neither hear nor see, nor benefit you at all?’”

“‘O my father, knowledge has come to me that has not come to you, so follow me, and I will guide you to a straight path.’”

“‘O my father, do not worship Satan. Indeed, Satan has been rebellious against the Most Merciful.’”

“‘O my father, I fear that a punishment from the Most Merciful may touch you, and you would become a companion of Satan.’”

“He said, ‘Do you reject my gods, O Abraham? If you do not desist, I will surely stone you. Leave me for a long while.’”

“He said, ‘Peace be upon you. I will ask my Lord to forgive you. He has always been gracious to me.’”

“‘I will withdraw from you and from what you invoke besides Allah, and I will invoke my Lord. I trust that I will not be disappointed in calling upon my Lord.’”

“When he withdrew from them and from what they worshipped besides Allah, We granted him Isaac and Jacob, and made each of them a prophet.”

“We bestowed upon them Our mercy and granted them lofty, honourable renown.”

Thus He, glorified be He, says:

“When he withdrew from them and from what they worshipped besides Allah.”

Yet these heretics say: “In every object of worship, we have worshipped none other than Allah.”

And the Exalted said:

“In his absence, the people of Moses made from their ornaments a calf in bodily form

---

## Page 6835

that lowed. Did they not see that it could neither speak to them nor guide them to any way? They took it as a god and were wrongdoers.”

“When they were overcome with remorse and realised that they had gone astray, they said, ‘If our Lord does not have mercy on us and forgive us, we will surely be among the losers.’”

Up to His words:

“Indeed, those who took the calf as a god will incur anger from their Lord and humiliation in the life of this world. Thus do We recompense those who fabricate falsehood.”

Abū Qilābah said: “This applies to every fabricator until the Day of Resurrection: Allah will humiliate him.”

All the Jahmī negationists are fabricators, as Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal said: “Their doctrine leads only to a fabrication against Allah.” These people are among the greatest fabricators of falsehood against Allah. Those who say that the Creator’s existence is the very existence of creation fabricate an even greater falsehood than those who say that He indwells creation.

These people regard as ignorant anyone who professes indwelling or union—that is, that the Creator has united with creation. Such union can only occur when two things are distinct and then one unites with the other, as the Christians speak of the union of the divine nature with the human nature. This can only be said of a particular entity. In these people’s view, however, there is no existence other than His that could unite with His existence.

They are among the most self-contradictory of people. They say, “There is nothing other than Him, nothing besides Him.” The Sab‘īniyyah say, “There is nothing but Allah,” instead of the Muslims’ declaration, “There is no god but Allah.” Then they say:

---

## Page 6836

“These veiled people do not see this.” But if there is nothing other than Him, nothing besides Him, then who is veiled, and who veils? Who is not veiled, and from what has he been veiled?

They have thus affirmed four things: people who are veiled, people who are not veiled, and something disclosed to the latter but concealed from the former. How can this be reconciled with their claim that there are neither two things nor two existences?

A trustworthy man told me that he said to al-Tilimsānī, “According to your position, then, there is no difference between a man’s wife, his mother, and his daughter?”

He replied, “Yes, they are all the same to us. But these veiled people said, ‘It is forbidden,’ so we said, ‘It is forbidden to you.’”

They were then asked: “Who addresses the veiled people? Is he they themselves, or someone other than them? If they are he himself, then he has forbidden it to himself when he claims that it is forbidden to them but not to him. If they are other than him, then he has affirmed two distinct entities, whereas according to them there is nothing other.”

These people have confused unity in kind with numerical unity. For one may say, “Existence is one,” just as one says, “Humanity is one, and animality is one,” meaning a single universal. But a universal is universal only in the mind, not in external reality. They supposed that this universal existed externally, then supposed that it was Allah. Yet no universal exists externally as a universal; it is universal only in the mind. If a universal is posited as existing externally, it is a part of particular entities and inheres in them; it is not distinct and self-subsisting. Thus, an animal’s animal nature and a human being’s human nature, whether conceived as particular or unrestricted, are attributes of that being. It is impossible for an attribute of something to be its creator. Even if

---

## Page 6837

its existence were posited as separate from what is perceptible, according to the view of those who affirm “Platonic Forms”—thus positing universal essences separate from the things they qualify, and claiming that they are eternal and beginningless, such as abstract humanity and abstract animality—this would be a false imagining. What they regard as abstract exists abstractly only in the mind; no abstract universal exists in external reality.

Even if an abstract universal were assumed to exist externally, namely what is called existence, it would encompass the existence of all things that have come into being just as it encompasses the existence of the Eternal. Such a universal cannot create anything, nor does it possess attributes of perfection exclusively. It cannot therefore be described as living, knowing, and powerful, since describing it thus would be no more warranted than describing it as powerless, ignorant, and dead. Yet the Creator must be living, knowing, and powerful. Glory be to Him! Exalted is He far above what the wrongdoers say!

Furthermore, even if this were assumed to be the Creator, it would be distinct from the existing, created particulars. Two existences would thus be established, each distinct from the other. One would have come into being and been created, while the other would be the Creator, distinct from what is created. The existence of particular entities cannot be denied.

However, one of these people may lose awareness of things concealed from him, just as he loses awareness of himself. He then supposes that whatever he does not witness has itself ceased to exist and vanished. This is not so. What has ceased and vanished is his witnessing of it, his knowledge of it, and his attention to it. What has ceased to exist and vanished is an attribute of this person; the existing things themselves remain as they were, unchanged. The absence of knowledge is not knowledge of the nonexistent, and the absence of what is witnessed is not witnessing nonexistence. Yet this state overtakes many who travel the spiritual path:

---

## Page 6838

one of them loses awareness of himself and other created beings. They may call this annihilation and overwhelming absorption. Yet this is the disappearance of his awareness of those created beings, not their actual disappearance.

When someone says, “What once was not has vanished, and what has always been remains,” the precise meaning—if he is truthful—is that his witnessing of what once was not has vanished, while his witnessing of what has always been remains. It does not mean that what once was not has itself vanished, for it still exists. Rather, when they no longer witness it, they imagine that it has ceased to exist in reality.

This is how some have fallen into doctrines of union and indwelling. One of them may remember Allah until that remembrance dominates his heart and he becomes completely absorbed in it. Nothing then remains present to his heart’s remembrance and witnessing except Allah, while his remembrance and witnessing of everything besides Him disappear. He thus imagines that all things have vanished and that he himself has vanished, until he imagines that he is Allah and that existence is Allah.

The error of Abū Yazīd and others like him belongs to this category, as when he said, “There is nothing in this robe but Allah.”

This has been discussed at length elsewhere, where it was explained that annihilation is used to refer to three things:

“First”: through worshipping Allah, he ceases to worship anything besides Him; and through loving and obeying Him

---

## Page 6839

and fearing Him, placing his hope in Him and relying upon Him, he ceases to love, obey, fear, place his hope in or rely upon anything besides Him. This is the reality of the monotheism with which Allah sent the messengers and revealed the Scriptures. It is the fulfilment of the testimony that none is worthy of worship except Allah. Devotion to anything besides Allah has vanished from his heart, while devotion to Allah alone remains. Love of anything besides Allah, fear of anything besides Allah and reliance upon anything besides Allah have vanished from his heart, while love of Allah, fear of Allah and reliance upon Allah remain.

This annihilation coexists with subsistence: the heart divests itself of worshipping anything besides Allah while adorning itself with worshipping Allah alone. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to a man:

“Say: ‘I have submitted to Allah and renounced all else.’”

This is the fulfilment of the testimony that none is worthy of worship except Allah, through negation together with affirmation: denying divinity to anything besides Him while affirming His divinity alone. For there is no deity in existence except Allah; nothing in existence deserves worship except Allah. This must therefore be firmly established in the heart, so that there is none whom the heart adores and worships except Allah alone. Every devotion to anything besides Allah must leave the heart, and devotion to Allah alone must become firmly established within it, since there is no deity but Allah alone.

This allegiance to Allah is coupled with disavowal of, and enmity towards, every object of worship besides Him and those who worship them. Allah Most High said concerning His intimate friend, peace be upon him:

“And when Abraham said to his father and his people, ‘I am free of what you worship, except the One who created me, for He will surely guide me.’ And he made it an enduring declaration among his descendants, so that they might return.”

And He said:

“Have you considered what you have been worshipping—you and your fathers

---

## Page 6840

of old? They are enemies to me, except the Lord of all worlds.”

Allah Most High said:

“You have indeed had an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disavow you and whatever you worship besides Allah. We reject you, and enmity and hatred have arisen between us and you forever, until you believe in Allah alone.’”

I said to some of their religious masters with whom I spoke: “Regarding the words of Allah’s intimate friend, ‘I am free of what you worship’—whom did he disavow? Did he disavow Allah Most High, when, according to you, none but Allah has ever been worshipped?”

Allah’s intimate friend disavowed everything they worshipped except the Lord of all worlds. Allah made him and those with him an excellent example for us—for whoever hopes in Allah and the Last Day. Allah Most High said:

“You have indeed had an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disavow you and whatever you worship besides Allah. We reject you, and enmity and hatred have arisen between us and you forever, until you believe in Allah alone’—except for Abraham’s words to his father, ‘I shall certainly seek forgiveness for you, but I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way.’ ‘Our Lord, in You we place our trust, to You we turn in repentance, and to You is the final return.

‘Our Lord, do not make us a trial for those who disbelieve, and forgive us, our Lord. You alone are the Almighty, the All-Wise.’

You have certainly had in them an excellent example for whoever hopes in Allah and the Last Day. Whoever turns away—Allah is indeed the Self-Sufficient, the Praiseworthy.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “The truest statement a poet ever made was the statement of Labīd:

---

## Page 6841

‘Indeed, everything apart from Allah is vain.’”

This confirms the words of Allah, exalted and Most High:

“That is because Allah is the Truth, and what they invoke besides Him is falsehood, and because Allah is the Most High, the Most Great.”

He Most High also said:

“That, then, is Allah, your true Lord. What lies beyond the truth except error? How, then, are you turned away?”

He, glorified be He, said:

“Everything will perish except His Face.”

A group of the early generations said: “Every deed is vain except what is done seeking His Face.” He, glorified be He, also said:

“Do not let them turn you away from Allah’s revelations after they have been sent down to you. Call people to your Lord, and never be among those who associate partners with Him.”

“And do not invoke another god alongside Allah.”

An ilāh (god) is a ma’lūh (one who is worshipped): one who deserves to be taken as a god—that is, worshipped. None deserves to be taken as a god and worshipped except Allah alone. Every object of worship besides Him, from His Throne to the depths of His earth, is a false object of worship. The form fi‘āl can carry the passive meaning of maf‘ūl, as in rikāb and himāl, meaning respectively “that which is ridden” and “that which is carried.”

While digging the trench, the Companions would chant:

“This is the load, not the load of Khaybar;
This, our Lord, is more righteous and purer.”

When it is said, “This is the imam,” it means someone who deserves to be followed, as Allah Most High said to Abraham:

“‘I will make you a leader for the people.’ He said, ‘And what of my descendants?’

---

## Page 6842

He said, ‘My covenant does not extend to the wrongdoers.’” Thus, His covenant concerning leadership does not extend to a wrongdoer. A wrongdoer must neither be followed in his wrongdoing nor be inclined toward, as Allah Most High said:

“Do not incline toward those who do wrong, lest the Fire touch you.”

Whoever follows someone unfit for leadership has wronged himself. What, then, of someone who sets up another god alongside Allah and worships one unworthy of worship? Allah Most High says:

“He does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.”

A group of speculative theologians erred by supposing that “ilāh” (god) had an active meaning. They understood divinity as power and lordship: the god is the one who has power and is the lord. They described His servants as ma’lūh, just as they are subject to His lordship.

Those who profess the oneness of existence disagree over various matters. Their leading authority, however, Ibn ‘Arabī, says:

“Entities are fixed in nonexistence, and the existence of the Real emanated upon them.”

For this reason, he said:

“Thus, through our being ma’lūh, we made Him a god.”

He therefore claimed that created beings made the Lord their god by being ma’lūh. By ma’lūh, he means those subject to lordship; their being ma’lūh rests on their entities being fixed in nonexistence. Their discourse contains countless statements of this kind that detract from divine lordship. Exalted is Allah, far above what the wrongdoers say.

The “correct understanding” is that Allah is the Creator of everything, and what is nonexistent is not a thing in external reality. Yet Allah knows what will be before it comes into being, writes it down, and may mention it

---

## Page 6843

and reports it; it is thus a cause in knowledge, mention, and writing, but not in external reality. As He said:

“His command, when He wills a thing, is only to say to it, ‘Be,’ and it is.”

Allah, glorified is He, is man’s Creator and Teacher. He is the One who “created man from a clinging clot,” and He is “the Most Generous,” “who taught by the pen,” “taught man what he did not know.”

Even supposing that “god” meant “lord,” it is He who makes a creature subject to His lordship. On this understanding, it would be He who makes the ma’lūh a ma’lūh. The creature subject to His lordship did not make Him a Lord. Rather, lordship is His attribute, and He is the One who created the creature and made it subject to His lordship. When that creature believes in the Lord, acknowledges His lordship, and declares it, it has taken Allah as its Lord, sought no lord besides Allah, and taken no other lord. As Allah Most High said:

“Say, ‘Should I seek a lord other than Allah, when He is the Lord of all things?’”

And Allah Most High said:

“Should I take as a protector anyone other than Allah, the Originator of the heavens and the earth?”

And He said:

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have submitted to Allah?”

He Himself is also the true God; there is no god besides Him. Thus, when a person worships Him without setting up another god alongside Him or taking any god besides Him, that person has affirmed His oneness. Allah Most High said:

“So do not invoke another god alongside Allah, lest you be among those punished.”

And Allah Most High said:

“Do not set up another god alongside Allah, lest you be left condemned and forsaken.”

Abraham said to his father Āzar:

“Do you take idols as gods? Indeed, I see you and your people in manifest error.”

A created being is not a god in itself. Rather, its worshipper has taken it as a god, made it a god, and called it

---

## Page 6844

a god. All of this is false and brings its practitioner no benefit; rather, it harms him. Likewise, appointing an ignorant person as an imam, mufti, or judge is invalid, for he is unfit to lead, issue fatwas, or judge. None besides Allah is fit to be taken as a god to be worshipped and invoked, for none besides Him creates or provides. He—glorified is He—has no one who can withhold what He gives or give what He withholds, nor can worldly prosperity avail its possessor against Him.

Whoever invokes someone who cannot hear his supplication, or who hears but does not answer him, has made a futile and misguided supplication. Everyone besides Allah either does not hear the supplicant’s prayer or hears it but does not answer him, for none besides Allah can do anything independently whatsoever. Allah Most High has said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those you claim to be gods besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession before Him is of no benefit except for those to whom He gives permission.”

Thus, none besides Allah owns anything, has a share in anything, or assists the Lord in anything. Someone among the angels, prophets, or righteous may, however, have the right to intercede. Yet intercession before Him is of no benefit except for those to whom He gives permission. He must therefore permit the intercessor to intercede and permit intercession on behalf of its intended recipient. Those besides Him have no power over intercession whatsoever. None besides Him is therefore fit to be a god who is worshipped, just as none is fit to be a creator and provider. There is no god but Him alone, without partner. His is the dominion and His is all praise, and He has power over all things.

---

## Page 6845

Section

One of the greatest causes of these people’s misguidance was their sharing the philosophers’ views and receiving teachings from them. Those philosophers are among the people furthest removed from reasoning on the basis of what the Messenger brought. For the Messenger was sent with clear proofs and guidance: he explains rational proofs and informs people of matters of the unseen that they cannot know through their own reason.

These proponents of philosophy claim that he imparted no knowledge to people, either through his reports or through his demonstrations. Rather, he merely addressed the masses in a manner intended to reform them, so that they would hold beneficial beliefs about the Lord and the Hereafter, even if those beliefs were false and unfounded. What their position really amounts to is that the prophets lie in what they report, but do so for a beneficial purpose. This precluded their seeking knowledge from the prophets’ reports. If those reports do not correspond to what they describe, how could they establish rational proofs for the truth of what they reported?

The speculative theologians—who say that the prophets report only the truth, yet follow a path other than theirs in rational matters—are innovators, despite acknowledging that the Quran contains rational proofs. What, then, of these heretics and fabricators? For this reason, they pay no attention to the Quran or its interpretation, nor to hadith or the words of the early generations. And if

---

## Page 6846

they learn any of this, it is only because the public is attached to it, allowing them to make a living among them by mentioning it—not because they inwardly believe what it entails. This is unlike the various groups of speculative theologians, who generally revere the Quran and its interpretation, despite their innovations.

Thus, when the Tatars seized Baghdad, al-Tūsī, who was Hulagu’s astrologer, took possession of people’s books, whether endowed or privately owned. He destroyed Islamic books, such as works of Quranic interpretation, hadith, jurisprudence, and spiritual exhortation, while keeping books on medicine, astrology, philosophy, and the Arabic language. These were the books he esteemed.

One man I knew was a Quran reciter and preacher, yet he revered these people and practised philosophical and magical disciplines in order to enlist the jinn’s service. Some devils had suggested to him that these people would take over the lands of Islam. He would say to one of our companions, “So-and-so, before long, logic, physics, mathematics, and metaphysics will be taught in this mosque—the congregational mosque of Damascus.” Then, to please him, he would add, “And Arabic as well.”

Yet Muslims needed Arabic only because the Messenger addressed them in it. If one turns away from that foundation, scholars of Arabic are like the pre-Islamic poets—the authors of the Seven Suspended Odes and others like them—fuel for the Fire.

---

## Page 6847

Section

Division and religious innovation first arose in Islam after the killing of ‘Uthmān and the ensuing split among the Muslims. When ‘Alī and Mu‘āwiya agreed to arbitration, the Khārijites objected, saying, “Judgment belongs to Allah alone,” and broke away from the Muslim community. ‘Alī sent Ibn ‘Abbās to debate with them, and half of them returned. The rest raided people’s livestock and deemed their blood lawful to shed. They killed Ibn Khabbāb and said, “We all killed him.” ‘Alī then fought them.

Their doctrine was founded on reverence for the Quran and a desire to follow it, but they departed from the Sunnah and the Muslim community. They did not consider it right to follow any Sunnah they thought contradicted the Quran, such as stoning and the minimum value required for the prescribed punishment for theft. Thus they went astray, for the Messenger knew best what Allah had revealed to him, and Allah had revealed to him the Book and wisdom.

They also considered it possible for the Prophet to be unjust, so they would not submit to his judgment or that of the rulers after him. Rather, they said that ‘Uthmān, ‘Alī, and their supporters had judged by something other than what Allah had revealed:

“Those who do not judge by what Allah has revealed are the disbelievers.”

On this and other grounds, they declared Muslims disbelievers. Their declarations of unbelief, and those of all other proponents of religious innovation, rest on two false premises:

The first is that this contradicts the Quran.

---

## Page 6848

And the second is that whoever contradicts the Quran becomes a disbeliever, even if he does so in error, or sins while acknowledging what is obligatory and what is forbidden.

At the opposite extreme, the Shī‘a went to excess regarding their imams, declaring them infallible and all-knowing. They made it obligatory to refer to them concerning everything the messengers brought. Thus, they heed neither the Quran nor the Sunnah, but rather the statements of those they suppose to be infallible. This ultimately led them to follow a nonexistent imam who has no reality. They were therefore further astray than the Khārijites: the latter refer to the Quran, which is true, even though they misunderstand it, whereas the former refer to nothing at all—only to someone nonexistent, with no reality. Moreover, they cling only to what is transmitted to them from certain deceased people, relying on an unverified report from someone who is not infallible. For this reason, they were the most mendacious of the sects. The Khārijites, by contrast, are truthful: their narrations are among the soundest, whereas those of the Shī‘a are among the most false.

Yet the principle the Khārijites exalt in their religion is separation from the Muslim community and deeming it lawful to shed Muslims’ blood and seize their wealth. The Shī‘a favor this too, but lack the power to carry it out. The Zaydiyya do so, while the Imāmiyya sometimes do so and sometimes say, “We do not kill except under the banner of an infallible imam.”

The Shī‘a have drawn into their following enemies of the religion, including heretics, the Bātiniyya, and others. For this reason, the heretics—such as the Qarmatians of Bahrain, who were among the most unbelieving of all creation, and the Qarmatians of the Maghreb and Egypt, who concealed themselves behind Shiism—advised infiltrating the Muslims through the gateway of Shiism, for they

---

## Page 6849

open the door to every enemy of Islam among the polytheists, the People of the Book, and the hypocrites. They are among those furthest removed from the Quran and hadith, as has been explained at length elsewhere.

The point is that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I am leaving among you two weighty things: the Book of Allah”—and he urged adherence to the Book of Allah—then said: “And my kindred, the members of my household. I remind you of your duty to Allah concerning my household,” repeating this three times.

Thus, he enjoined the Muslims to care for them; he did not appoint them as imams to whom the Muslims were to refer. The Khārijites professed adherence to the Book of Allah, while the Shīʿa professed adherence to the Prophet’s household, yet neither followed what they professed to follow. The Khārijites contradicted the Sunnah, which the Quran commands people to follow, and declared believers disbelievers, although the Quran commands loyalty to them. For this reason, Saʿd ibn Abī Waqqās understood the following verse as applying to them:

“But He misleads thereby none except the defiantly disobedient,”

“who break Allah’s covenant after it has been confirmed, sever what Allah has commanded to be joined, and spread corruption on earth.”

They began pursuing the ambiguous passages of the Quran, interpreting them incorrectly, without understanding their meaning, being firmly grounded in knowledge, following the Sunnah, or consulting the community of Muslims who understand the Quran. As for the ways in which the Shīʿa contradict the Prophet’s household, these are very numerous and have been explained at length elsewhere.

---

## Page 6850

Section

Then, toward the end of the era of the Companions, the Qadariyya emerged. The Khārijites had concerned themselves with Allah’s revealed rulings: His commands and prohibitions, the promises and threats associated with them, the status of those who complied with them and those who opposed them, and who was a believer or a disbeliever. These are the “questions of religious designations and rulings.” They were called the Muhakkima because they engaged wrongfully in questions of judgment. When a man said, “Judgment belongs to Allah alone,” they would say, “He is a muhakkim,” meaning someone who engages in discussion of Allah’s judgment. Thus, those people engaged wrongfully in discussing Allah’s revealed law, whereas the Qadariyya engaged wrongfully in discussing His decree.

The root of their error was their assumption that divine decree contradicts revealed law. They therefore split into two factions. One faction revered the revealed law—its commands and prohibitions, promises and threats—and upheld following what Allah loves and is pleased with, and abandoning what He hates and what incurs His displeasure. They thought that this could not be reconciled with divine decree. Thus, they severed what Allah had commanded to be joined and broke Allah’s covenant after it had been confirmed, just as the Khārijites severed what Allah had commanded to be joined: the harmony between the Book, the Sunnah, and those who adhere to the Muslim community. They separated the Book from the Sunnah, separated the Book from the Muslim community, divided the Muslims, and severed

---

## Page 6851

what Allah had commanded to be joined. The same was true of the “Qadariyya,” who split into two factions. “One faction” gives precedence to revealed law and thus rejects and denies divine decree, or denies part of it. The “other faction” gives precedence to divine decree and thus inwardly denies revealed law or denies its reality, saying: “In reality, there is no difference between what Allah has commanded and what He has forbidden; all are alike. The same applies to His allies and His enemies, and to what He has said He loves and what He has said He hates. He has merely differentiated between identical things by sheer will, commanding one thing and forbidding its like.”

Thus, they denied the distinction and separation between monotheism and associating partners with Allah, between faith and disbelief, between obedience and disobedience, and between the lawful and the unlawful. Conversely, although the former faction acknowledged these distinctions, they denied what unites all things. They denied that Allah has power over all things, and some of them denied that Allah knows all things. They denied that He is the Creator of everything, that whatever He wills comes to pass and whatever He does not will does not come to pass, and that Allah does whatever He wills. They ascribed independent creative power to others besides Allah and affirmed partners who created as He creates, just as the Magians did. They believed that affirming His commands and prohibitions was possible only by attributing inability or ignorance to Him, and that He could not be described as benevolent and generous unless He were deemed incapable; otherwise, He would necessarily be miserly.

Likewise, the “determinist Qadariyya” said: “He cannot be regarded as all-knowing and all-powerful

---

## Page 6852

except by attributing folly and injustice to Him.”

These people denied His wisdom and justice, while the others denied His power and will—or His power, will, and knowledge. One group resembled the Magians in associating partners with Him in His lordship, since they made someone other than Him a creator. The other resembled the polytheists who make no distinction between worshipping Him and worshipping others, but permit worshipping others just as they permit worshipping Him, saying:

“If Allah had willed, we would not have associated partners with Him”—to the end of the verse.

Their affirmation of Allah’s oneness extends no further than that of the polytheists: affirming His oneness in lordship. As for His exclusive right to worship—which encompasses His commands and prohibitions, and the fact that Allah loves what He commands and hates what He forbids—they deny it. Consequently, they follow their desires more readily than the Muʿtazila, are more given to associating partners with Allah, and are more permissive. The ultimate position of their theologians and devotees is to permit idol worship and to maintain that the spiritual knower neither regards a good deed as good nor an evil deed as evil, as stated by the author of Manāzil al-Sā’irīn (The Stations of the Wayfarers).

As for idol worship, their later figures openly advocated it. Al-Rāzī composed a work on it, while Ibn ʿArabī, Ibn Sabʿīn, and others like them explicitly declare it permissible and condemn those who denounce it. Yet they contradict themselves in this.

The fundamental premise of the “Qadariyya” is that His power and wisdom cannot both be affirmed: if He were able, He would have done something other than what He did; since He did not, this indicates that He was unable. They said:

“His wisdom is affirmed just as His judgment is affirmed, because denying this would entail folly and injustice, from which He is free. This is unlike what He is unable to do, for He is excused when He does not do it

---

## Page 6853

and is therefore not to be blamed for it.”

The “compulsionists” said: “Rather, His power is affirmed without wisdom. He cannot act for a wise purpose, since that applies only to someone who needs to act, whereas He is free of need. There is neither justice nor injustice; rather, whatever He can do is just. Among actions, there is neither good that ought to be commanded nor evil that ought to be forbidden, nor any right or wrong. Rather, He may command anything and forbid anything.”

Those among them who followed this reasoning through to its conclusion rejected the revealed Law altogether and rejected prophethood. Yet they cannot avoid commanding some things and forbidding others, for this is inescapable for all created beings. Those who follow the prophets, however, command what benefits themselves and others, and forbid what harms themselves and others. Those who oppose the prophets inevitably command what is harmful and forbid what is beneficial, thereby deserving punishment in this world and the Hereafter.

As for someone among them who acknowledges prophethood but inwardly rejects the revealed Law and says, “The spiritual knower neither regards a good deed as good nor an evil deed as evil,” he becomes a hypocrite whose outward professions contradict his inward convictions. He says, “The revealed Law is for the asylum.” This is why they are called “Bātiniyya” (esotericists), just as the heretics have been called “Bātiniyya”: both conceal beliefs contrary to what they outwardly profess, inwardly setting aside the commands and prohibitions brought by the Messenger.

The ultimate outcome for the compulsionist Jahmiyya is to be either polytheists both outwardly and inwardly, or hypocrites who conceal their polytheism. Hence they think evil of Allah, believing that He will not grant victory to Muhammad

---

## Page 6854

and his followers, as the Most High says:

“And that He may punish the hypocrites, men and women, and the polytheists, men and women, who harbor evil thoughts about Allah. Upon them is an evil turn of fortune. Allah is angry with them, has cursed them, and has prepared Hell for them—an evil destination.”

They cling to His words, “He is not questioned about what He does,” and to the fact that He “does whatever He wills.”

Thus, when the polytheist Tatars and the People of the Book gained ascendancy, many among their devout worshippers and scholars sided with the polytheists and the People of the Book and apostatized from Islam, either inwardly and outwardly, or inwardly alone. Such a person would say that he was in accord with reality and with the divine will. To those who revered the messengers and would not agree to reject them, they began arguing that their practices—polytheism, departing from the revealed Law, allying themselves with the polytheists and the People of the Book, embracing their religion, and fighting alongside them against the Muslims—were commanded by the Messenger.

At times, their devils would bring them something they made them imagine was written in light, declaring that the Messenger had commanded them to fight alongside the unbelievers against the Muslims because the Muslims had been disobedient.

When it became apparent that the polytheists and the People of the Book had protectors accompanying them from among Muslim men—the “men of the unseen”—and that these men possessed extraordinary powers implying that they were friends of Allah, the people of learning divided into three groups. One group denied that such men existed. Yet people had seen them, and this was established through those who had witnessed them or received accounts from trustworthy eyewitnesses. If these deniers see them or become certain of their existence, they submit to them.

---

## Page 6855

Another group recognized them and appealed to divine decree, believing that there was an inward path to Allah other than the path of the prophets.

A third group could not accept that friends of Allah could lie outside the Messenger’s authority, so they said: “The Messenger himself supplies aid to both factions—to these and to those.” These people revere the Messenger but are ignorant of his religion and revealed Law, whereas the preceding group permits following a religion other than his and a path other than his.

These three views were current in Damascus when Acre was conquered. It subsequently became clear that these people were followers of devils, that the “men of the unseen” were jinn, and that those accompanying the unbelievers were devils. Any human who sided with them was one of their kind: a human devil, among the enemies of the prophets. As the Most High says:

“And thus We have appointed for every prophet enemies—devils from humankind and jinn—who inspire one another with embellished speech to deceive.”

The cause of this misguidance was the failure to distinguish between the friends of the Most Merciful and the allies of Satan. Its root was the doctrine of the Jahmiyyah, who place all created beings on an equal footing, making no distinction between what Allah loves and what incurs His wrath. Many things then occurred that would take too long to describe. When Ghāzān, who had embraced Islam, came to Damascus, further matters came to light, and it became apparent that

---

## Page 6856

the Yūnusiyyah had apostatized and become unbelievers alongside the other unbelievers.

One of their shaykhs came to see me, admitted his apostasy from Islam, and related many matters to me. When he mentioned how they justified themselves by what had reached them as the Messenger’s instructions, I said to him:

“Suppose that Muslims such as the people of Baghdad had sinned, and that there were a dozen or so prostitutes in Baghdad. The army of unbelieving polytheists who came was worse than those women. Those women committed fornication willingly, whereas those polytheists seized tens of thousands of the Muslims’ free women and concubines against their will, turned them from Islam to unbelief, and openly promoted polytheism, idol worship, Christianity, and veneration of the cross. The Muslims were left subjugated among the polytheists and the People of the Book, while sins were committed on a far greater scale than before. Would Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—command this and approve of it?”

The matter became clear to him, and he said, “No, by Allah!” He also told me about the shaykhs who had apostatized from Islam when the polytheists’ devils forced them to renounce their faith inwardly, tormenting them if they refused. I said, “This happened because of the weakness of their faith, their affirmation of Allah’s oneness, and the support they perceived as coming from the Messenger. Otherwise, devils have no authority over the hearts of those who affirm Allah’s oneness.”

This man and others like him did not believe that these beings were devils. Rather, they believed them to be human “men of the unseen” whom Allah had entrusted with directing affairs. I therefore explained to them that the “men of the unseen” were jinn, as the Most High says:

“And there were men from humankind who sought refuge with men from the jinn, and they increased them in

---

## Page 6857

burden.” Anyone who supposes that they are human does so out of ignorance and error. Human beings are perceptible—that is, they are witnessed and seen. A human may sometimes conceal himself, but he is not perpetually hidden from human sight. The jinn are different, as Allah says:

“He and his tribe see you from where you cannot see them.”

Other shaykhs related an account concerning Shaykh Muhammad ibn al-Sakrān: when Hulagu, the king of the polytheists, entered Baghdad, Ibn al-Sakrān saw a shaven-headed shaykh, looking like one of the shaykhs of religion and the spiritual path, holding Hulagu’s horse by its bridle. He related:

“When I saw him, I disapproved of this and considered it outrageous that a Muslim shaykh should lead the horse of the king of the polytheists to kill Muslims. I said, ‘You there!’—or words to that effect. He then said, ‘Do you command this on orders?’ Or he said to him, ‘Is this being done on orders?’ or, ‘Did you do this on orders?’ I said, ‘Yes, on orders.’”

Ibn al-Sakrān fell silent, satisfied with this answer. This was because he knew little about distinguishing between the allies of the Most Merciful and the allies of Satan. He supposed that whatever the shaykhs were commanded to do in their hearts came from Allah; that when someone said, “My heart has narrated to me on the authority of my Lord,” Allah Himself was speaking privately to him; and that someone who said, “You have received your knowledge from the dead, who received it from the dead, whereas we have received our knowledge from the Ever-Living, who never dies,” was indeed speaking the truth. This is even further astray than claiming to have no need of the prophets or their mediation.

The answer is to ask him, “On whose orders do you command this?” If he says, “On Allah’s orders,”

---

## Page 6858

he should be asked, “Do you mean Allah’s command, conveyed through His Messenger and revealed in the Quran, or a command that came into your heart?”

If he says, “The former,” his falsehood becomes evident. Allah’s commands to His Messenger do not include bringing unbelievers—polytheists and People of the Book—to kill Muslims, take them captive, and seize their wealth because of sins those Muslims have committed; or turning the land into a place where idols are worshipped, bells are rung, reciters of the Quran and scholars of Islamic law are killed, and the Najsiyya—the scholars of the polytheists—and Christian priests are honored; or other such things. For these people are far more hostile to Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and are like the Arab polytheists who fought him at Uhud. Those Muslims, by contrast, are sinners among his nation. Although there are many hypocrites among them, those hypocrites conceal their hypocrisy.

If he says, “A command that came into my heart,” he has not lied. But he should be asked, “How do you know that this comes from the Most Merciful? Why could it not be Satan who commanded you to do this? You know that what comes into the hearts of the polytheists and the People of the Book comes from Satan.”

If he falls back on affirming Allah’s oneness in His lordship, arguing that everything occurs by His will, he should be told, “In that case, what Satan, the polytheists, and the People of the Book do is also done on command.” There is no doubt that it occurs by the command pertaining to creation and divine decree, to which all creation is subject. But whoever acts solely on the basis of this command, rather than the Messenger’s command, is merely one of the devils among humankind and jinn. He deserves Allah’s punishment in this world and the Hereafter, worships something other than Allah, and follows his own desires. He is among those concerning whom Allah said:

“I will surely fill Hell with you and with those who

---

## Page 6859

follow you, all together.” He is also among those of whom Satan said:

“By Your might, I will surely mislead them all, except Your chosen servants among them.”

Allah said:

“Indeed, you have no authority over My servants, except those among the misguided who follow you.”

And the Most High said:

“Indeed, he has no authority over those who believe and put their trust in their Lord. His authority is only over those who take him as an ally and those who associate others with Allah through him.”

And the Most High said:

“Indeed, We have made the devils allies of those who do not believe. When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our fathers doing it, and Allah has commanded us to do it.’ Say, ‘Indeed, Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

How, then, can you command polytheism and unbelief and give unbelievers—polytheists and People of the Book—power over Muslims? The killing of Muslims by unbelievers is not something Allah commands, just as He does not command indecency. Indeed, this is among the gravest indecencies, if “indecency” is understood to encompass everything profoundly reprehensible, so that all evil and reprehensible deeds fall within its scope.

There was also in Baalbek, in Syria, one of the leading shaykhs—Shaykh Uthmān, the shaykh of Dayr Nā‘is. The protector of the Christian Franks would visit him riding a lion, meet with him in seclusion, and speak to him confidentially, saying, “O Shaykh Uthmān, I have been entrusted with guarding their pigs.” Uthmān and his followers would excuse him for this, believing that Allah had commanded him to do it, just as He had commanded al-Khidr to do what he did. Likewise, Ibn al-Sakrān and others like him excused the protectors of the polytheist Tatars.

The answer to this is the same as the answer to the previous case. He should be asked, “Has Allah Most High entrusted you with this?

---

## Page 6860

The One who revealed the religion through His Prophet, who commanded loyalty to the Muslims and forbade taking the Jews and Christians as allies—indeed, He commanded you to hate them and strive against them to the best of your ability—has He commanded you to undertake the guarding of their pigs?” If he says this, his falsehood becomes evident. But if he says, “Rather, it is a command cast into my heart,” he would not be lying. He should then be told: “This is a command from Satan, not the command of the Most Merciful which He revealed in His Books and sent His messengers to convey. It is, however, among the things He has brought into being and decreed, like the polytheism of those who said:

‘If Allah had willed, neither we nor our fathers would have associated partners with Him.’”

Some among these people suppose that the men through whom the unbelievers—polytheists and People of the Book—receive support are allies of Allah, and that they are not required to follow the Messenger, like the angels assigned to the children of Adam, attending them in succession. I said to one of their shaykhs: “Muhammad was sent to both communities, humans and jinn; he was not sent to the angels. Thus, any human or jinn who does not believe in him is an enemy of Allah, not an ally of Allah. The angels are a different case.”

He should then be told: “The angels do not assist the unbelievers in committing sins or fighting the Muslims; it is only the devils who assist them in that. The angels may, however, be charged with their creation, their provision, and the recording of their deeds, for none of that is sinful.” Thus, the answer lies in distinguishing between them and the angels in these two respects.

---

## Page 6861

It has become clear that they belong among the devils, not the angels. This shaykh and his father were protectors of the unbelievers. His father was called “Muhammad al-Khālidī,” a name associating him with a devil whom he kept close, called Shaykh Khālid. They claim that this devil is a human, one of the “men of the unseen.”

A trustworthy informant related to me that he used to say, “The prophets have ruined the path.” By my life, they certainly have ruined the path of the devils—the devils among humans and jinn!

These shaykhs who love the Muslims, yet maintain allegiance to shaykhs who ally themselves with the polytheists and serve as protectors of the unbelievers, believing them to be allies of Allah, share with those men a fundamental error: they regard satanic wonders as belonging to the same category as miraculous gifts from the Most Merciful. They have failed to distinguish between the allies of the Most Merciful and the allies of Satan. As Allah Most High says:

“And whoever turns a blind eye to the reminder of the Most Merciful, We appoint for him a devil to be his companion.”

Both groups have turned a blind eye to the reminder that the Most Merciful revealed—the Book and the Sunnah—and to the spirit that Allah revealed to His Prophet, which Allah made a light by which He guides whomever He wills of His servants.
