Through it, the allies of the Most Merciful are distinguished from the allies of Satan. Nor have they distinguished between the prophets’ signs and miracles and the extraordinary feats of sorcerers and soothsayers. For this is “the doctrine of the Jahmiyyah determinists,” and all these people share this doctrine. They do not hold that Allah loves what He commands and hates what He forbids. Rather, they hold that He loves everything He has decreed and ordained

---

## Page 6862

and is pleased with it. Thus, all matters become equal in their eyes; the only distinction they recognize is some extraordinary feat. Whoever performs such a feat, they regard as an ally of Allah and submit to him—whether by following him, agreeing with him and loving him, or accepting his state without either loving or hating him. For their hearts no longer retain enough faith to recognize right and reject wrong in this matter.

It is authentically reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever among you sees a wrong, let him change it with his hand. If he cannot, then with his tongue; and if he cannot, then with his heart—and that is the weakest level of faith.”

In a narration recorded by Muslim:

“Whoever strives against them with his hand is a believer; whoever strives against them with his tongue is a believer; and whoever strives against them with his heart is a believer. Beyond that, there is not a mustard seed’s weight of faith.”

The dead among the living are those who neither recognize right nor reject wrong. In the hadith of Hudhayfah recorded in Sahīh Muslim:

“Temptations are presented to hearts as a mat is woven, reed by reed. Any heart that rejects them receives a white mark, and any heart that absorbs them receives a black mark, until hearts become of two kinds: a white heart, like smooth stone, which no temptation harms as long as the heavens and earth endure; and a black, dusky heart that neither recognizes right nor rejects wrong, except what accords with the desires it has absorbed.”

Thus, these devout ascetics who worshipped Allah according to their own opinions, spiritual tastes, and experiences, rather than

---

## Page 6863

His commands and prohibitions, ultimately end up following their desires:

“And who is more astray than one who follows his own desires without guidance from Allah?”

This is especially so when their real position is that of “the Jahmī determinists.” They see all created things as equally subject to Allah’s will, without distinguishing between what Allah loves and is pleased with and what He hates and is angered by. Allah loves what is right and hates what is wrong. When they fail to distinguish between the two, black marks are imprinted on their hearts until their hearts become blackened. Right then becomes whatever they desire and love, and whatever they experience and savour spiritually; wrong becomes whatever they feel inclined to hate and whatever repels their hearts. They are like the polytheists who were:

“Turning away from the reminder, like startled donkeys fleeing from a lion.”

Thus, among these people and their followers are those who recoil from the Quran and the revealed law, just as startled donkeys flee from archers and lions. Hence they are described as recoiling whenever they are told, “The Chosen One said…”

Shaykh Ibrāhīm ibn Mi‘dād would say to those he encountered among such people, including the Yūnusiyyah and the Ahmadiyyah: “You swine! You sons of swine! I find among you not the slightest regard for Allah and His Messenger!”

“Rather, each one of them desires to be given scrolls spread open.”

Each of them wants his heart to convey to him from his Lord, so that he may receive directly from Allah without the Messenger’s mediation:

“When a sign comes to them, they say, ‘We will never believe until we are given the like of what Allah’s messengers were given.’ Allah knows best where to place His message.”

A fuller discussion of this belongs elsewhere.

The point here is that the doctrine of “the Qadarī, Jahmī determinists” is even more contrary

---

## Page 6864

to what the messengers brought than is the doctrine of the deniers. For this reason, they did not openly profess this in the time of the early generations. Rather, whenever the light of prophethood grew dimmer, they disclosed the true nature of their doctrine. It is of the same kind as the doctrine of the polytheists who rejected the messengers, and its ultimate outcome is associating partners with Allah and rejecting the messengers. These together constitute the essence of disbelief, just as affirming Allah’s oneness and believing the messengers constitute the essence of faith. Thus they have sided with those guilty of outright disbelief among the polytheists and the People of the Book. A fuller discussion of these matters belongs elsewhere.

The point here is that “the Qadarī determinists” are akin to the polytheists, just as “the deniers” are akin to the Magians. In actual fact, the determinists acknowledge nothing beyond divine power and will, while the deniers deny Allah’s all-encompassing power and will, claiming to affirm wisdom and justice. In reality, both groups deny wisdom, justice, will and power, as has been explained at length elsewhere.

Those people appeal to His words:

“He is not questioned about what He does,”

and:

“Allah does whatever He wills.”

Allah stated this to affirm His power, not to deny His wisdom and justice. Rather, He—glorified be He—made clear that He does whatever He wills: no one can oppose Him when He wills something, and He has the power to do whatever He wills. This is unlike a created being, who wills many things but cannot do them.

For this reason, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said in an authentic hadith:

“Let none of you say, ‘O Allah, forgive me if You will; O Allah, have mercy on me if You will,’ for no one can compel Allah. Rather, let him be resolute in his request.”

---

## Page 6865

This is because one only says, “Do such and such if you wish,” to someone who might do it under compulsion, doing what he does not want in order to avoid the harm of being compelled. But no one can compel Allah Most High, so He does only what He wills. Thus His words:

“Indeed, Allah does whatever He wills,”

and:

“He forgives whom He wills and punishes whom He wills,”

and similar statements affirm His power to do whatever He wills. This refutes the Qadarī deniers, who say that He has not willed everything that occurs, but wills only obedience. Yet, although He willed obedience, it did not occur on the part of those who disobeyed Him. According to them, He has no power to make a servant obedient or disobedient.

These verses, which the determinists cite as evidence, demonstrate the falsity of the deniers’ doctrine. Likewise, the verses cited by the deniers—which establish that He is a just Judge, that He does not wrong anyone even by an atom’s weight, that He did not create creation in vain, and other such truths—demonstrate the falsity of the determinists’ position. Neither set of verses, however, establishes the correctness of either group’s position. Rather, the evidence cited by each group demonstrates the falsity of the other’s doctrine, while both positions are false.

This is what the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, prohibited in the hadith recorded in the Musnad and elsewhere, part of which is also found in Sahīh Muslim. It is reported from ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him:

“Came out to his Companions while they were disputing about divine decree. One was saying, ‘Has Allah not said such and such?’ and another was saying, ‘Has Allah not said such and such?’ His face was as though pomegranate seeds had burst upon it, and he said, ‘Is this what you were commanded to do? Or is this what you were called to—to set parts of Allah’s Book against others?’”

This is why Ahmad said, in one of his debates with someone who was setting the verses

---

## Page 6866

against one another: “We have been forbidden to do this.” Whoever rejects texts that others cite as evidence does not believe in them; rather, he believes only in what he himself cites, thus becoming one of those who believe in part of the Book and disbelieve in another.

This is the condition of those who follow their whims: they disagree over the Book, oppose the Book, and agree in opposing the Book. They have all abandoned some of its texts—the very texts that would reconcile those positions. They have thus become as Allah described the People of the Book:

“And from those who say, ‘We are Christians,’ We took their covenant, but they forgot a portion of what they had been reminded of. So We stirred up enmity and hatred among them until the Day of Resurrection.”

When people abandon part of what Allah has revealed, enmity and hatred arise among them, since there is no longer a unifying truth that they share. Rather:

“They divided their religion among themselves into sects, each faction rejoicing in what it had.”

All these groups possess only as much truth as accords with the Messenger: whatever they have retained of his revealed teaching, both what he taught and what he commanded. As for what they have introduced, it is all misguidance, as he, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Beware of newly introduced matters, for every religious innovation is misguidance.”

They may regard such an innovation as more important than the revealed teaching they have accepted, treating it as their “rational principles.” Such is the case with the Qadarīs, both the determinists and the deniers. Both groups regard the theological arguments they have introduced concerning fundamental principles—which they call rational matters—as more important than what they have received through revelation. Thus, the Mu'tazilah treat both matters of belief and matters of command as rational matters, including obligations prescribed by revealed law

---

## Page 6867

, though they also say that revealed law has made them obligatory. Their treatment of this, however, contains confusion that this is not the place to discuss. The same applies to the arguments they have introduced concerning matters of belief, such as proving that the world had a beginning by arguing from accidents and the bodies they necessarily imply. They deny the divine attributes and divine decree, calling this “monotheism and justice.”

Jahm ibn Safwān and his followers go further in negation, denying the divine names as well as the attributes. They are the foremost proponents of determinism. The Ash'arīs agreed with them on determinism, but disputed with them in merely verbal terms by affirming acquisition and the capacity for it. They regard these rational principles—knowledge of what necessarily belongs to the Lord, what is impossible for Him, and what acts it is possible for Him to perform—as the greatest and noblest branches of knowledge. They believe that through them they have surpassed the Companions, and that the Prophet did not teach them to the Companions: either because he left the community to deduce them, because the Companions were too occupied with jihad to study them, or because he told them things on this subject that they did not transmit, without occupying them with the proofs because they were engaged in jihad.

These are the “rational principles” upon which they and those who agree with them rely, such as al-Qādī Abū Ya'lā, Abū al-Ma'ālī, and Abū al-Walīd al-Bājī, following al-Qādī Abū Bakr and others like him. He and his followers contest the views of 'Abd al-Jabbār and others like him, just as al-Ash'arī and others like him contested those of Abū 'Alī and Abū Hāshim.

---

## Page 6868

All the rational principles invented by both these groups are false according to both reason and revealed law, even though each group considers them among the greatest matters of religion and gives them precedence over the principles of revealed law. In this respect, they are like the devotees, ascetics, faqīrs, and Sufis who exalt extraordinary feats of satanic origin and prefer them to prescribed acts of worship. Yet those prescribed acts of worship constitute their share of Islam, while those extraordinary feats are all false, even though they regard them as greater than acts of worship. They go so far as to say:

“The Sufi’s final stage is the jurist’s starting point, and the jurist’s final stage is the ecstatic’s starting point.”

Likewise, the author of Manāzil al-Sā’irīn (The Stations of the Wayfarers) mentions three degrees in every chapter. The first, which they regard as the lowest, outwardly conforms to revealed law. The second may or may not conform to it, while the third generally contradicts it, especially concerning “monotheism,” “annihilation,” “hope,” and similar matters. What they have innovated is greater in their estimation than those matters in which they have followed the messengers. Many worshippers prefer their voluntary acts of worship to the performance of obligatory duties. This is widespread, and Allah knows best.

All praise belongs to Allah alone. May Allah bless our master Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them abundant peace. All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of all worlds.

---

## Page 6869

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked about a group of people who profess spiritual poverty. They claim that the Quran has an inner meaning, and that this inner meaning has another inner meaning, continuing through seven levels. In support of this, they relate a hadith in which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, supposedly said:

“The Quran has an inner meaning, and that inner meaning has another inner meaning, continuing through seven levels.”

They interpret the Quran in ways other than those known from the Companions, the Successors, and the leading jurists. They claim that ‘Alī said, “If I wished, I could fill so many camel-loads with commentary on the Opening of the Book.” They say, “This is knowledge peculiar to us, for it is knowledge bestowed directly by Allah.”

They also make statements to the effect that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, gave each group instructions suited to it: he instructed some to retain their wealth and others to spend it, some to earn a living and others to refrain from earning.

They say, “Our shaykhs have mentioned this in al-‘Awārif and other works by authoritative scholars.” Sometimes they mention that

---

## Page 6870

Hudhayfah knew the names of the hypocrites, knowledge which the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had imparted specifically to him. They also cite the hadith of Abū Hurayrah:

“I memorized two bags’ worth of knowledge.”

They relate a statement from Abū Sa‘īd al-Kharrāz, who said:

“Those who know Allah have treasuries in which they have deposited extraordinary knowledge. They speak of it in the language of endless eternity and describe it in the language of beginningless eternity.”

They also say that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Some knowledge is like a hidden treasure, known only to those who know Allah. When they speak of it, none reject it except those who are deluded about Allah.”

Are their claims correct or not? May my master explain their statements to us, for your servant has come across a passage by one of the scholars mentioning that al-Wāhidī said:

“Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān al-Sulamī compiled a book which he called Haqā’iq al-Tafsīr (The Realities of Quranic Interpretation). If it is authentically attributed to him, then he has disbelieved.”

I have examined this book and found that this group’s statements come from it or resemble its contents. What is my master’s view of this?

Is it authentically reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, “The Quran has an inner meaning,” and the rest of the hadith? They interpret it according to their own spiritual tastes and experiences, which are rejected by Islamic law. Give us your ruling, and may you be rewarded.

The shaykh—may Allah be pleased with him—answered:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of all worlds. As for the aforementioned hadith, it is one of the fabricated hadiths that have not been transmitted by anyone

---

## Page 6871

among the scholars, nor is it found in any of the hadith collections. However, it is narrated from al-Hasan al-Basrī, either as his own statement or as a report attributed to the Prophet with an omitted intermediary:

“Every verse has an outward aspect, an inward aspect, a limit, and a point of ascent.”

The expressions “outward knowledge and inward knowledge” and “people of the outward and people of the inward” have become widespread in many people’s speech. These expressions have come to encompass both truth and falsehood. This has been discussed at length elsewhere, but here we shall outline some of its main points.

When someone speaks of “the inward,” he may mean knowledge of inward matters, such as the knowledge and states within people’s hearts, and knowledge of the unseen matters conveyed by the messengers. Alternatively, he may mean hidden knowledge—that is, knowledge concealed from the understanding of most people, or from the understanding of those who confine themselves to the outward, and so forth.

As for the first meaning, there is no doubt that some knowledge concerns outward matters, such as the deeds of the bodily limbs, while some concerns inward matters, such as the deeds of the heart. Some concerns the observable—what people perceive through their senses—while some concerns the unseen—what lies beyond their sensory perception.

The foundation of faith is belief in the unseen, as Allah Most High says:

“Alif Lām Mīm.”
“This

---

## Page 6872

Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for the God-fearing.”
“Those who believe in the unseen.”

The unseen in which one believes consists of the general matters reported by the messengers. This includes belief in Allah, His names and attributes, His angels, Paradise, and Hell. Belief in Allah, His messengers, and the Last Day thus entails belief in the unseen, for messengership itself is a matter of the unseen. In detail, this means belief in Allah, His angels, His Books, His messengers, and the Last Day, as Allah Most High mentions in His words:

“Rather, the truly righteous are those who believe in Allah, the Last Day, the angels, the Book, and the prophets.”

He also says:

“Whoever disbelieves in Allah, His angels, His Books, His messengers, and the Last Day has indeed strayed far away.”

Knowledge of the states of the heart—such as knowledge of sound and corrupt beliefs and intentions; knowledge concerning knowing Allah, loving Him, being sincere to Him, fearing Him, relying upon Him, placing hope in Him, loving and hating for His sake, being content with His judgment, and turning to Him; and knowledge of the praiseworthy and blameworthy qualities of the soul, such as generosity, modesty, humility, arrogance, self-admiration, boastfulness, and conceit, and similar fields of knowledge concerning inward matters within hearts and the like—may be called “inward knowledge,” meaning knowledge of inward matters. It is therefore the object of knowledge that is inward. As for the outward knowledge, it is manifest, expressed in speech and writing. This is attested by the Quran, the Sunnah, and the words of the early generations and their followers. Indeed, most verses of the Quran concern this

---

## Page 6873

kind of knowledge; for Allah sent down the Quran as “a healing for what is in the hearts, and guidance and mercy for the believers.” Indeed, this knowledge concerns the very foundations of religion: belief in the heart is the foundation of the tongue’s speech, and the heart’s actions are the foundation of the limbs’ actions. The heart is the king of the body, as Abū Hurayra—may Allah be pleased with him—said:

“The heart is a king, and the limbs are its soldiers. When the king is good, its soldiers are good; when the king is corrupt, its soldiers are corrupt.”

In the two Sahīh collections, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, is reported to have said:

“Indeed, there is a piece of flesh in the body: when it is sound, the rest of the body is sound, and when it is corrupt, the rest of the body is corrupt. Indeed, it is the heart.”

Whoever neither knows what rectifies his inward state and what corrupts it, nor seeks to rectify his heart through faith and warding off hypocrisy, is a hypocrite if he outwardly professes Islam. Both the believer and the hypocrite outwardly profess Islam: Islam is outwardly manifest, whereas faith is in the heart. As reported in al-Musnad, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Islam is outwardly manifest, and faith is in the heart.”

The statements of the Companions and their successors, and the hadiths and reports concerning this subject, are far, far more numerous than those concerning leasing, pre-emption, menstruation, and ritual purification. Yet this knowledge is outwardly manifest and available, expressed in speech and recorded in books. The more knowledgeable someone is about the affairs of the heart, the more knowledgeable he is about this subject and the meanings of the Quran and hadith. People generally find these things within themselves through direct experience and inward feeling, so they become

---

## Page 6874

perceptible to them through their inward sense. Yet people differ enormously in their realization of the realities of faith. Those at the higher level know the condition of those below them, but not vice versa—just as, among the inhabitants of Paradise, those above descend to those below, while those below do not ascend to those above. The learned person understands the ignorant person because he was once ignorant, whereas the ignorant person does not understand the learned person because he has never been learned.

Thus, among the inward realities of faith and the realities of the unseen tidings conveyed by the messengers, there are things known only to a select few. This knowledge is therefore hidden in two respects: its subject matter is hidden, and the knowledge itself is hidden, being unknown to most people. Moreover, discussion of this knowledge admits truth and falsehood to an extent not found elsewhere. Whatever accords with the Quran and Sunnah is true, and whatever contradicts them is false, just as with discussion of outward matters.

Section

If, however, “inward knowledge” means knowledge hidden from most people, or from some of them, then it falls into two categories:

“The first” is hidden knowledge that contradicts outward knowledge.

“The second” does not contradict it.

---

## Page 6875

As for the first, it is false. Anyone who claims inward knowledge, or knowledge of inward matters, that contradicts outward knowledge is in error: either an unbelieving heretic or an ignorant, misguided person. The second, however, is like discourse concerning outward knowledge: it may be true or false. If inward knowledge does not contradict outward knowledge, its falsity cannot be established on the grounds that it contradicts what is outwardly known. If it is known to be true, it is accepted; if it is known to be false, it is rejected; otherwise, judgment is withheld.

Examples of inward teachings that contradict what is outwardly known include the claims of the esotericist Qarmatians among the Ismā‘īlīs and Nusayrīs, and others like them among the philosophers, extremist Sufis, and speculative theologians who agree with them. The worst of these are the Qarmatians, who claim that the Quran and Islam have an inward meaning that contradicts their outward meaning.

Thus they say that the prescribed “prayer” is not this prayer, or that this prayer is prescribed only for ordinary people. As for the elite, prayer means knowing our secrets; “fasting” means concealing our secrets; and “pilgrimage” means travelling to visit our sanctified shaykhs.

They say that, for the elite, “Paradise” means enjoying pleasures in this world, while the “Fire” means adhering to religious laws and submitting to their burdens. They say that the “Beast” which Allah will bring forth for people is the scholar who speaks with knowledge in every age; that “Isrāfīl,” who blows the Trumpet, is the scholar who breathes his knowledge into hearts until they come alive; that “Gabriel” is the Active Intellect from which beings emanate; and that the “Pen” is the First Intellect

---

## Page 6876

which the philosophers claim is the first originator; that the stars, moon, and sun which Abraham saw are the Soul, the Intellect, and the Necessary Existent; that the four rivers which the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, saw on the night of his Ascension are the four elements; and that the prophets he saw in heaven are the celestial bodies. Thus, Adam is the moon, Joseph is Venus, and Idrīs is the sun—and other such notions.

Many speculative theologians and Sufis have adopted many of these people’s teachings. However, those Qarmatians outwardly profess Rāfidism, while inwardly harbouring sheer unbelief. Most Sufis and speculative theologians are not Rāfidīs who denounce the Companions as sinners or unbelievers. Among them, however, are those who, like the Zaydīs, regard ‘Alī as superior to Abū Bakr. Others regard ‘Alī as superior in inward knowledge, following the approach of al-Harbī and others like him. They claim that ‘Alī had greater inward knowledge, that this knowledge is superior in that respect, and that Abū Bakr had greater outward knowledge.

Their position is the opposite of that held by the discerning scholars and leading authorities among the Sufis, who agree that the person most knowledgeable in inward knowledge is Abū Bakr al-Siddīq. Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah have agreed that Abū Bakr is the most knowledgeable member of this nation in both inward and outward matters, and more than one scholar has reported consensus on this.

These esotericists may interpret:

“And We have recorded all things in a clear register”

as referring to ‘Alī. They interpret the Most High’s words:

“May the hands of Abū Lahab perish, and may he perish!”

as identifying the two hands with Abū Bakr and ‘Umar, and His words:

“Then fight the leaders of disbelief”

as referring to Talha and al-Zubayr

---

## Page 6877

and “the accursed tree in the Quran” as referring to the Umayyads.

As for the Sufi esotericists, they say that Pharaoh in the Most High’s words, “Go to Pharaoh,” is the heart, and that the cow in “Allah commands you to slaughter a cow” is the soul. Those other esotericists say that it is ‘Ā’ishah. They and the philosophers interpret Allah’s speaking to Moses as what emanates to him from the Active Intellect or some other source. They take “removing the two sandals” to mean abandoning this world and the Hereafter. They interpret “the tree from which Moses was addressed,” “the sacred valley,” and similar expressions as states experienced by the heart when it acquires knowledge.

Among those who followed this course were the author of *The Niche of Lights* and others like him. This was among the things for which Muslims most strongly censured him. They said, “*The Healing* made him ill,” and, “He plunged into the depths of the philosophers’ thought, then sought to emerge but could not.” Some people challenge the authenticity of these books, saying that they were falsely attributed to him. Others say that he later renounced them. This is the most plausible view, for he explicitly declared the philosophers unbelievers on certain issues and misguided on an even greater number of issues. He also explicitly stated that their method does not lead to the desired goal.

The esotericist philosophers interpret angels and devils as faculties of the soul. They interpret what people are promised in the Hereafter as parables intended to explain the pleasure and pain experienced by the soul after death, rather than as affirming independently existing realities through which it experiences bliss or suffering.

On this subject, the writings of many later Sufis contain statements the like of which are not found among their leading authorities and predecessors, just as the writings of many later adherents of

---

## Page 6878

speculative theology and rational inquiry contain statements of this kind that are not found among their leading authorities and predecessors. Despite their misguidance and ignorance, these later figures claim to possess greater knowledge and understanding than the early generations of the Muslim community and their predecessors. They went so far as to declare existence to be one, as did Ibn ‘Arabī, the author of al-Fusūs, and others like him. Entering through this door, they ultimately abandoned all reason and religion.

Yet they claim that the earlier masters—such as al-Junayd ibn Muhammad, Sahl ibn ‘Abd Allāh al-Tustarī, Ibrāhīm al-Khawwās, and others—died without understanding Allah’s oneness. They censure al-Junayd and others like him for distinguishing between the Lord and the servant, as in his statement: “Affirming Allah’s oneness means distinguishing that which came into being from that which is beginningless.”

By my life, their conception of oneness, in which they identify the existence of the creature with the existence of the Creator, is among the gravest forms of heresy condemned by the rightly guided masters. Those masters knew it to be false, so they rejected it, warned people against it, and instructed them to distinguish between the Lord and the servant, the Creator and the creature, and the Beginningless and that which came into being. They taught that affirming Allah’s oneness means knowing that the Lord is separate and distinct from His creatures: nothing of His Essence is within His creatures, and nothing of His creatures is within His Essence.

They further claim to know Allah better than the messengers do, and that the messengers acquire their knowledge of Allah only from their lamp-niches. They interpret the Quran in ways that accord with their false esoteric teachings. For example, concerning His words,

“Because of their sins,”

they say that these were what carried them forward, so that they drowned in the oceans of knowledge of Allah. They also claim that ‘adhāb (punishment) is derived from ‘udhūbah (sweetness), and they say that

---

## Page 6879

Noah’s words concerning his people are praise expressed in the language of blame. They interpret His words, Exalted is He,

“Indeed, those who disbelieve—it is all the same to them whether you warn them or do not warn them—they will not believe,”

as meaning that they do not believe in outward knowledge. Rather,

“Allah has sealed their hearts,”

so they know none but Him,

“and their hearing and their sight,”

so they hear from none but Him and see none but Him. For there is none other than Him, and thus they see none but Him.

Concerning His words,

“Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him,”

they say that this means He has predestined it, because nothing exists besides Him. Thus, it is inconceivable that anyone else should be worshipped. Everyone who worshipped idols or the calf therefore worshipped none but Him, since there is no other. Such are these interpretations and explanations, which every believer, Jew, and Christian knows with certainty to contradict what the messengers brought, including Moses, Jesus, and Muhammad—may Allah bless them all.

In summary, this subject falls into two categories:

The first is where the proposed meaning is false because it contradicts what is known. Such a meaning is false in itself, so any evidence adduced for it must also be invalid, since no proof can establish a falsehood as true.

The second is where the meaning is true in itself, but they seek to establish it from the Quran and hadith through wording that was not intended to convey that meaning. This is what they call “allusions.” Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān’s Haqā’iq al-Tafsīr (The Realities of Quranic Interpretation) contains many examples of this.

---

## Page 6880

As for the “first category,” it occurs frequently in the writings of the Qarmatians and the philosophers who oppose the Muslims concerning the foundations of their religion. Whoever knows that Allah is pleased with the earliest forerunners and that they are pleased with Him knows that everything these people say to the contrary is false. Whoever acknowledges that the five daily prayers are obligatory upon everyone so long as they retain their reason knows that anyone who interprets a text as exempting some people from this obligation has fabricated a falsehood. Likewise, whoever knows that wine and indecent acts are forbidden to everyone so long as they retain their reason knows that anyone who interprets a text in a way that makes these things lawful for some people is fabricating a falsehood.

As for the “second category,” it often causes confusion for some people. The meaning may be correct, since the Quran and Sunnah substantiate it; the question, however, is whether the wording they cite actually conveys it. This falls into two subdivisions:

The “first” is to claim that this meaning is intended by the wording. This is fabricating a falsehood against Allah. Thus, whoever says that what is meant by His words, “slaughter a cow,” is the soul; by “Go to Pharaoh,” the heart; by “and those with him,” Abū Bakr; by “stern against the disbelievers,” ‘Umar; by “merciful among themselves,” ‘Uthmān; and by “You see them bowing and prostrating,” ‘Alī—has spoken falsely about Allah, whether deliberately or mistakenly.

The “second subdivision” is to treat this as a matter of drawing lessons and reasoning by analogy

---

## Page 6881

rather than as a meaning conveyed by the wording. This is a form of analogy: what the jurists call analogy, the Sufis call allusion. This can be sound or unsound, just as analogy can.

Thus, suppose someone hears Allah’s words:

“None touch it except the purified,”

understands this to refer to the Preserved Tablet or a written copy of the Quran, and says: “Just as the Preserved Tablet, on which the letters of the Quran are written, is touched only by a pure body, so the meanings of the Quran are tasted only by pure hearts—the hearts of the God-fearing.” This is a sound meaning and a sound lesson to draw. Accordingly, this is reported from a number of the early generations. Allah Most High says:

“Alif Lām Mīm.”

“This is the Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for the God-fearing.”

He also says:

“This is a clear statement to mankind, and guidance and admonition for the God-fearing.”

And:

“By it Allah guides those who seek His good pleasure to the ways of peace.”

There are other similar passages.

Likewise, someone may cite the saying:

“Angels do not enter a house containing a dog or a person in a state of major ritual impurity,”

and draw from it the lesson that the realities of faith do not enter a heart containing things that defile it, such as arrogance and envy. In this, he is correct. Allah Most High says:

“Those are the ones whose hearts Allah does not wish to purify.”

Allah Most High also says:

“I will turn away from My signs those who act arrogantly upon the earth without right. Even if they see every sign, they will not believe in it. If they see the way of right guidance, they will not take it as their way; but if they see the way of error, they will take it as their way.”

There are other similar passages.

The book Haqā’iq al-Tafsīr (The Realities of Quranic Exegesis), by Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān al-Sulamī, contains three kinds of material:

---

## Page 6882

First: reports whose attribution to those quoted is weak, such as most of what he transmits from Ja‘far al-Sādiq, much of which is falsely attributed to him. Most of the reports in the book stop at Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān himself. Experts have criticized Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān’s own transmission, to the point that, when al-Bayhaqī narrated from him, he would say: “He narrated to us from the original record of what he had heard.”

Second: reports that are authentically transmitted, but whose narrator was mistaken in what he said.

Third: authentic reports from someone whose statement is correct.

Every meaning that contradicts the Quran and Sunnah is false, and the argument supporting it is untenable. As for a meaning that accords with the Quran and Sunnah but is not what the wording intends, interpreting the wording by it is an error. If, however, it is mentioned by way of allusion, drawing a lesson, or analogy, it may be sound or unsound.

It is thus clear that whoever explains or interprets the Quran or hadith contrary to the interpretation known from the Companions and their Successors is fabricating lies against Allah, deviating concerning Allah’s signs, and distorting words from their proper places. This opens the door to heresy and impiety. That it is false is a matter of basic, indisputable knowledge in Islam.

As for statements reported from some of them that admit of more than one meaning, such as one of them saying:

“If

---

## Page 6883

I wished, I could provide loads of commentary on the Opening of the Book,” and so forth. If such a statement is authentically reported from the person to whom it is attributed, such as ‘Alī or someone else, it offers no evidence for an inward meaning that contradicts the outward meaning. Rather, it would concern a sound inward meaning consistent with the sound outward meaning. As explained earlier, if “inward meaning” refers to something that does not contradict the known outward meaning, it may be true or false. It should, however, be understood that more lies have been attributed to ‘Alī and his household—especially Ja‘far al-Sādiq—than to other Companions. Indeed, the Ismā‘īliyyah and Nusayriyyah attribute their doctrines to him, as do the Mu‘tazilah.

Likewise, a group of Sufis claim that al-Hasan al-Basrī was his companion, and that ‘Alī entered the mosque and saw al-Hasan preaching among the storytellers. He asked him, “What makes religion sound?” He replied, “Scrupulousness.”

He asked, “And what corrupts it?” He replied, “Greed.” So he allowed him to remain and expelled the others.

Yet experts in transmitted reports agree that al-Hasan was not a companion of ‘Alī and learned nothing directly from him. Rather, he learned from his companions, such as al-Ahnaf ibn Qays, Qays ibn Sa‘d ibn ‘Abbād, and others like them. Al-Hasan did not preach during ‘Alī’s time, nor even during Mu‘āwiyah’s time; he only began doing so later.

People were already attributing lies to ‘Alī during his lifetime, to the point that people would question him about this. As established in the two Sahīhs, he was asked:

“Do you have a document from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, which you read?”

He replied, “No, by Him who split the seed and created the living soul, except this sheet.” It contains the ages of camels, the release of captives, and the stipulation that a Muslim is not to be killed for a disbeliever.

In another wording: “Were you entrusted

---

## Page 6884

by the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family and grant them peace, with anything he did not entrust to others?”

He replied, “No.” In another wording: “Except for an understanding of His Book that Allah grants a servant.”

As for “divinely bestowed knowledge,” there is no doubt that Allah opens the hearts of His God-fearing friends and His righteous servants to insights He does not grant others, because their hearts are free of what He dislikes and they follow what He loves. This is as ‘Alī said: “Except for an understanding of His Book that Allah grants a servant.” A transmitted report states: “Whoever acts upon what he knows, Allah grants him knowledge of what he did not know.”

The Quran indicates this in several places, as in His words:

“If they had done what they were admonished to do, it would have been better for them and would have established them more firmly. Then We would have granted them a great reward from Us, and We would have guided them to a straight path.”

He thus declares that Allah guides whoever does what he is commanded to do to a straight path. Allah Most High also says:

“Through it Allah guides those who seek His pleasure to the ways of peace.”

And Allah Most High says:

“As for those who are guided, He increases them in guidance and grants them their piety.”

He also says:

“They were young men who believed in their Lord, and We increased them in guidance.”

And Allah Most High says:

“This is the Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for the God-fearing.”

And Allah Most High says:

“This is enlightenment for mankind, and guidance and mercy for people who have certainty.”

And Allah Most High says:

“This is enlightenment from your Lord, and guidance and mercy for people who believe.”

He also declares that pursuing what He dislikes turns people away from knowledge and guidance, as in His words:

“When they deviated, Allah caused their hearts to deviate.”

And His words:

“They swear by Allah their most solemn oaths that if a sign came to them, they would surely believe in it. Say, ‘Signs are only with Allah.’ And what will make you realize

---

## Page 6885

that when it comes, they will not believe?”

“And We will turn their hearts and eyes away, just as they did not believe in it the first time, and leave them wandering blindly in their transgression.”

That is: what will make you realize that when it comes, they will not believe in it, and We will turn their hearts away? In other words, they abandon faith, and We turn their hearts away because they did not believe the first time. That is, how do you know that both these things will not happen then?

Whoever understands the meaning of the verse will recognize the error of those who say that anna (“that”) means la‘alla (“perhaps”) and find the reading with an initial a-vowel problematic. Indeed, he will then recognize that it is better than the reading with an initial i-vowel. This is a broad subject.

People fall into three groups regarding this matter: two extremes and a middle course. One group claims that asceticism, purification of the heart, and discipline of the soul alone necessarily produce knowledge, without any other means.

Another group says that these have no effect; rather, knowledge is produced by knowing the scriptural or rational evidence.

The middle course is that these practices are among the greatest aids to attaining knowledge. Indeed, they are a prerequisite for acquiring much knowledge, but they are not sufficient on their own. Something else is indispensable: either knowledge of the evidence in matters that can only be known through it,

---

## Page 6886

or a correct conception of the two terms of a proposition in matters of non-inferential knowledge.

As for the beneficial knowledge through which people are saved from the Fire and attain happiness, it can only be acquired by following the scriptures brought by the messengers. Allah Most High says:

“When guidance comes to you from Me, whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor fall into misery.”

“But whoever turns away from My remembrance will have a life of hardship, and We shall raise him blind on the Day of Resurrection.”

“He will say, ‘My Lord, why have You raised me blind when I used to see?’”

“He will say, ‘Thus it is: Our signs came to you, but you forgot them; likewise, today you will be forgotten.’”

“And thus We recompense whoever transgresses and does not believe,” and so on.

Allah Most High also says:

“Whoever turns away from the remembrance of the Most Merciful, We appoint for him a devil who becomes his companion.”

Whoever thinks that guidance and faith can be attained merely by pursuing knowledge without acting upon it, or merely through practice and asceticism without knowledge, has gone astray. More astray than either is someone who, in pursuing knowledge and understanding, follows the path of the philosophers and speculative theologians without testing it against the Quran and Sunnah or acting as knowledge requires; or someone who, in practice and asceticism, follows the path of the philosophers and Sufis without testing it against the Quran and Sunnah or assessing practice in light of knowledge.

The latter have turned away from knowledge and revealed law, while the former have turned away from practice and revealed law. Each group has thus gone astray in these two respects. They have diverged greatly, until one group came to resemble the Jews who incurred divine wrath, and the other the Christians who went astray. Indeed, among both groups are some who have become worse

---

## Page 6887

than the Jews and Christians, such as the Qarmatians, the Ittihādiyya, and others like them among the heretical philosophers.

Section

As for the statement that “the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, assigned to each group what suited them,” and so forth, it admits of two interpretations.

If it means that prescribed deeds vary from person to person according to their circumstances, then there is no doubt about this. What the poor are commanded to do is not the same as what the wealthy are commanded to do; nor are the commands for the sick the same as those for the healthy. What is commanded in times of adversity differs from what is commanded when blessings are bestowed. A menstruating woman is not commanded to do the same as a woman who is ritually pure, nor are rulers commanded to do the same as their subjects. Thus, Allah’s commands to His servants may vary according to their circumstances, even as they may share the same foundation of faith in Allah, affirmation of His oneness, and belief in His Books and messengers.

If, however, it means that the Sharia itself varies, and that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, addressed Zayd with words contradicting what he said to ‘Amr, or disclosed to one person something contradicting what he disclosed to another—as the liars relate: “‘Ā’ishah asked him

---

## Page 6888

‘Have you seen your Lord?’ He replied, ‘No.’ Abū Bakr asked him, and he replied, ‘Yes.’” And the claim that “he gave two contradictory answers to a single question because the two questioners’ circumstances differed”—all this is the speech of liars and fabricators; indeed, it is the speech of heretical hypocrites. For the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“It is not fitting for a prophet to give a treacherous glance.”

This hadith appears in Sunan Abī Dāwūd and elsewhere:

“In the year of the Conquest, he had declared the lives of several men forfeit, including Ibn Abī Sarh. ‘Uthmān brought him to pledge allegiance to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him. He turned away from him twice or three times, then accepted his pledge. He then said, ‘Was there no man of sound judgment among you who, seeing me turn away from this man, would kill him?’ One of them said, ‘Why did you not signal to me with your eyes, O Messenger of Allah?’ He replied, ‘It is not fitting for a prophet to give a treacherous glance.’”

This emphatically affirms the complete agreement between his outward and inward states, his private and public conduct: he does not conceal anything contrary to what he outwardly displays, as scheming hypocrites habitually do.

There is no doubt that the Qarmatians and philosophers like them say that he outwardly displayed the opposite of what he concealed, and that he addressed the common people with statements by which he intended something contrary to what he led them to understand, for their own good, since he could reform them only by this means. Ibn Sīnā, the authors of the Epistles of the Brethren of Purity, and others like them among the philosophers and esoteric Qarmatians made this claim. Indeed, Ibn Sīnā and his household were followers of al-Hākim, the Qarmatian ‘Ubaydid ruler in Egypt. Just as their position is among the most flagrant expressions of disbelief, so too is their ignorance among the greatest.

---

## Page 6889

For if that were so, people of reason and intelligence would inevitably discover it. Once they knew it, their colluding to conceal it would be impossible in the ordinary course of events, just as their colluding to lie would be impossible. Just as universal collusion in lying is ordinarily impossible, so too is collusion in concealing something that people have strong motives and compelling reasons to explain and disclose. This applies especially to knowledge of such momentous matters, which people have the strongest motives and reasons to discover and discuss.

Do you not see that the esotericists and others like them concealed the opposite of what they outwardly presented to people, pursued every means of doing so, and colluded in it to whatever extent Allah willed, until their true nature became obscure even to many of their followers? Yet despite this, all intelligent people, supporters and opponents alike, discovered their true nature and wrote books exposing their secrets and lifting their veils. Those who knew their hidden reality had no inward respect for them, no trust in what they reported, and no commitment to obeying their commands. The same applies to anyone who displays anything of this kind.

Whoever follows this path loses the trust of those who know his true nature, both in what he reports and in what he commands. Everything he has said to people is then undermined, for whenever he addresses them, they consider it possible that he intends something other than what he outwardly conveys. They therefore trust neither his reports nor his commands, and his entire undertaking falls into disorder. His intention may be to reform them, but the result is grave corruption instead. Indeed, everyone who agrees with him must likewise outwardly display the opposite of what he conceals, as do the followers of those who have taken this path among the Qarmatians

---

## Page 6890

—the esotericists—and others. You will find that every one of their adherents inevitably reveals that his outward stance contradicts what he inwardly conceals. Their secrets are thereby exposed and their veils torn away, making them among the worst disbelievers.

If the messengers concealed the opposite of what they outwardly conveyed, then either others could discover this discrepancy or they could not. If discovering it were impossible, anyone claiming it would be a liar and a fabricator. Thus, the claim of these heretical philosophers, Qarmatians, and their like is invalid. If discovering it were possible, some people would learn that the inward reality contradicted the outward appearance. There would be no fixed limit to the number who could know this: once one person knew it, he would teach another, who would teach yet another, until it became widespread and openly known.

For this reason, those who hold such a belief about the prophets—such as these esotericists among the philosophers, Qarmatians, and their like—turn away from the true substance of his reports and commands. They believe neither in the inward truth of what he reported nor in what he commanded. Rather, their disobedience to his commands and disregard for his reports become apparent to everyone. You will find no believer who thinks well of them. Indeed, their wickedness and hypocrisy become apparent even to ordinary believers, let alone those of greater understanding.

Moreover, when someone is like this, his closest followers know his inward reality better than anyone else, and knowing it necessarily leads to an inward loss of commitment. Whoever knows the condition of the Prophet’s closest followers—Allah bless him and grant him peace—such as Abū Bakr, ʿUmar, and the other earliest believers, knows that they were foremost among people in affirming the inward and outward truth of his message and in their obedience

---

## Page 6891

to him, in private and in public. None of them held any belief about his reports or commands that contradicted the apparent meaning of what he had explained to them, indicated to them, and guided them towards. Thus, none of the Companions interpreted any of his statements contrary to what they indicated, whether concerning what Allah revealed about His names and attributes or what He revealed about events after death. Such interpretations arose only among those whom the leading scholars regarded as people of hypocrisy and the doctrine of union, such as the Qarmatians, the philosophers, and the Jahmiyyah, who deny the realities of the divine names and attributes.

To complete this discussion, you should know that the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—never singled out any of his Companions for instruction in religious knowledge that he intended to conceal from others. A man might, however, ask a question that could not be answered, and he would respond with something beneficial to him. For example:

> A Bedouin asked him about the Hour, although its timing is unknown. He replied, “What have you prepared for it?” The man said, “I have not prepared many deeds for it, but I love Allah and His Messenger.” He said, “A person will be with those whom he loves.”

He thus answered him by addressing the purpose of knowing about the Hour. He did not address his Companions in words they could not understand; rather, some understood his words more fully than others. Thus, in the two Sahīh collections, Abū Sa‘īd reports:

> The Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace—said, “Allah gave a servant the choice between this world and the Hereafter, and that servant chose what is with Allah.” Abū Bakr wept and said, “Rather, we would ransom you with our lives and our wealth, O Messenger of Allah!” The people began to marvel

---

## Page 6892

that the Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace—had mentioned a servant whom Allah had given the choice between this world and the Hereafter. He said: “The Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace—was himself the one given that choice, and Abū Bakr understood him better than any of us.”

The Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—thus mentioned an unspecified servant without identifying him, and nothing in his wording indicated his identity. Yet Abū Bakr, through his thorough understanding of the Messenger’s intentions, knew that the Messenger was that servant. Thus, Abū Bakr was not singled out from the others for an inner meaning that contradicted the apparent meaning. Rather, his understanding agreed with it and did not contradict what the Prophet’s words conveyed or meant.

As for the report transmitted by certain liars, attributing to ‘Umar the statement:

“The Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—and Abū Bakr would converse, while I was like a man of the Zanj between them.”

This is among the most obvious fabrications. No Muslim scholar has transmitted it in any scholarly work. Its falsehood is plain, for ‘Umar is the best of this community after Abū Bakr. He was one inwardly addressed and inspired, upon whose tongue and heart Allah placed the truth. He is the foremost of those in this community who are addressed and inspired. If he was present, hearing the words, yet understood the conversation no better than a man of the Zanj, could anyone else conceivably have understood it better than he did? What, then, of someone who never heard the Messenger’s words, but merely asserts that the meanings he claims are those intended? Such an assertion would not be accepted even without evidence against it; how, then, when evidence establishes the falsehood of the person making it?

As for the report concerning Hudhayfah, it is established in the Sahīh: “Hudhayfah was

---

## Page 6893

aware of the secret that no one else knew.” This was what the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—had confided to him during the year of Tabūk concerning the identities of the hypocrites. He related that a group of hypocrites had intended to loosen the girth of the Messenger of Allah’s camel at night so that he would fall from his mount and die. This was revealed to him, and Hudhayfah was nearby, so he secretly told him their names. It is said that ‘Umar would not offer the funeral prayer over anyone unless Hudhayfah did so.

None of this involves any of the truths of religion, nor any hidden meaning that contradicts the apparent meaning. Allah has described the hypocrites and related their deeds in His Book, so much so that Sūrat Barā’ah (Disavowal) was called “the Exposer” because it exposed the hypocrites. It was also called al-Muba‘thirah and given other names. Yet the Quran did not name particular individuals. If someone knows that certain individuals are among the hypocrites thus described, this is comparable to his knowing that certain individuals are among the believers promised Paradise. The Prophet’s declaration—Allah bless him and grant him peace—that Abū Bakr, ‘Umar, and others will be in Paradise is thus comparable to his declaration that those people were hypocrites. If this is considered hidden knowledge, it is hidden knowledge that agrees with the apparent meaning, confirms it, and corresponds to it.

Its counterpart in the sphere of commands is what is called “verifying the applicability of the ruling”: the Lawgiver makes a ruling contingent upon a particular attribute, and we ascertain that this attribute is present in a particular individual. An example is His command to call two upright men as witnesses, without specifying particular individuals. When we know that this man possesses

---

## Page 6894

uprightness, we know that this particular person possesses the uprightness mentioned in the Quran. Likewise, Allah has forbidden intoxicants and gambling. When we know that this drink made from sorghum and honey is an intoxicant, we know that it falls within this text. Our knowledge of particular believers and particular hypocrites belongs to this same category. This is part of the reality to which the Quran refers. Only Allah knows this in its entirety, for Allah knows every believer and every hypocrite, the degrees of their faith and hypocrisy, and the state in which their lives will end.

As for the Messenger, Allah Most High has said:

“Among the Bedouins around you are hypocrites, and among the people of Medina are those who persist in hypocrisy. You do not know them; We know them. We will punish them twice, then they will be returned to a tremendous punishment.”

Thus Allah discloses whatever He wills of this to His Messenger and to whomever He wills among His servants.

As for the hadith of Abū Hurayrah, it is authentic. He said:

“I memorized two bags’ worth of knowledge from the Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace. One of them I have disseminated among you; as for the other, had I disseminated it, you would have cut this throat.”

Yet none of this involves any hidden meaning that contradicts the apparent meaning, nor even any of the truths of religion. Rather, that bag contained reports of the battles and tribulations that would occur. The battles are wars between Muslims and disbelievers, while the tribulations are conflicts among Muslims.

---

## Page 6895

For this reason, ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Umar said:

“If Abū Hurayrah had told you that you would kill your caliph and do such and such, you would have said, ‘Abū Hurayrah has lied.’”

Disclosing such things is something kings and their supporters dislike, since it foretells changes in their rule. This is further clarified by the fact that Abū Hurayrah only embraced Islam in the year of Khaybar. He was therefore neither among the earliest Muslims nor among those who took the Pledge of Divine Pleasure. Other Companions knew more about the realities of religion than he did. The Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—would relate a hadith to him and others, and they would all hear it. Yet Abū Hurayrah retained hadith better than any of them, owing to a blessing he received through the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—for:

“One day, the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—was speaking to them and said, ‘Which of you will spread out his garment, so that he will forget nothing he has heard?’ Abū Hurayrah did so.”

It has also been reported:

“He used to divide the night into three parts: a third for prayer, a third for sleep, and a third for studying hadith.”

No one has ever transmitted from Abū Hurayrah any hadith that accords with the Bātiniyyah (esotericists), or any hadith that contradicts the known, apparent teachings of religion. It is evident that, had he possessed anything of this kind, someone would inevitably have transmitted some of it from him. Rather, the reports transmitted from him through so many channels as to establish certainty all affirm the apparent teachings of religion. He narrated hadiths concerning Allah’s attributes, the descriptions of the Last Day, and the proper fulfilment of acts of worship that accord with the principles of the people of faith and contradict the claims of the people of falsehood.

---

## Page 6896

As for what is related from Abū Sa‘īd al-Kharrāz and others like him on this subject, what Abū Tālib mentions in his book and what others relate, the statements of certain shaykhs thought to affirm an inward meaning that contradicts the apparent meaning, and similar passages found in the writings of Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī or others—the answer to all this is as follows:

What is known to have come from the Messenger is conveyed by a trustworthy transmitter from an infallible speaker. Whatever contradicts it must be either a report from an unreliable transmitter or the statement of someone who is not infallible. Much of what is attributed to these men is falsely ascribed to them. In what is authentically reported from them, they were sometimes right and sometimes wrong. Most of their established statements employ broad, ambiguous expressions. Even had these been the words of the infallible Messenger, they would not have contradicted the established ruling. How much less, then, when they are the words of someone who is not infallible!

Abū al-Fadl al-Falakī compiled a book of the sayings of Abū Yazīd al-Bistāmī, which he entitled “Light from the Sayings of Tayfūr.” It contains much that was undoubtedly falsely attributed to Abū Yazīd al-Bistāmī. It also contains some errors made by Abū Yazīd—may Allah have mercy on him—and some good statements of his. Everyone’s statements may be accepted or rejected, except those of the Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Anyone who is told that Abū Yazīd, or another of the shaykhs, said to his disciples:

---

## Page 6897

“If you allow anyone from Muhammad’s community to enter the Fire, I disown you”—whereupon another challenged him, saying, “I told my disciples, ‘If you allow anyone from Muhammad’s community to enter the Fire, I disown you’”—and who believes this report about Abū Yazīd or another shaykh, then admires it and regards his spiritual standing as exalted, has shown his profound ignorance or hypocrisy.

For if he knows what the Messenger foretold—that some who commit major sins will enter the Fire, and that the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—will be the first whose intercession on their behalf is accepted, after intercession has been sought from the great messengers, such as Noah, Abraham, Moses and Jesus, who will decline and offer their excuses—yet believes that the disciples of Abū Yazīd or another shaykh can prevent anyone from the community from entering the Fire, or that they themselves can bring out everyone who enters it, then he has disbelieved what the truthful and trustworthy Messenger foretold on the strength of a story whose transmitter lied, or whose original speaker was mistaken, if he was not deliberately lying. If, however, he does not know what the Messenger foretold, then he is among the most ignorant of people concerning the foundations of faith.

A Muslim must therefore hold firmly to the Quran and Sunnah and strive to attain certain knowledge of what the Messenger reported and commanded. He must then not abandon what is clear and known for what is ambiguous and unknown. That would be like someone travelling to Mecca along a known road which will undoubtedly take him there if he follows it, only to turn away from it onto an unknown road which he neither knows nor knows where it ends. Such is the case of someone who turns away

---

## Page 6898

from the Quran and Sunnah to the words of someone whose agreement or disagreement with them he cannot ascertain. As for someone who opposes the Quran and Sunnah with statements that contradict them, he is like a traveller who leaves the known road to Mecca and takes the road to Cyprus, hoping to reach Mecca by it. Such is the condition of someone who abandons what is known from the Quran and Sunnah for contradictory statements made by Zayd or ‘Amr, whoever they may be. Everyone’s statements may be accepted or rejected, except those of the Messenger of Allah—Allah bless him and grant him peace. In this regard, I have witnessed astonishing things whose number is known only to the One who knows what hearts conceal.

As for the reported hadith:

“Some knowledge is like a hidden treasure: none knows it except those who have knowledge of Allah, and when they mention it, none rejects it except those who are heedless concerning Allah.”

Shaykh al-Islam Abū Ismā‘īl al-Ansārī narrated it in his book entitled The Distinction between Those Who Affirm the Divine Attributes and Those Who Negate Them. In it, he included reports concerning the divine attributes: authentic and singular reports, reports with connected and disconnected chains, and reports stopping at the Companions. Abū Hāmid al-Ghazālī also mentioned it in his books. The latter interprets it in a manner consistent with his own statements, in which he inclines towards views resembling those of the philosophers and others who deny the divine attributes.

Shaykh al-Islam related that his teacher, Yahyā ibn ‘Ammār, used to say:

---

## Page 6899

“What is meant by this is the hadiths concerning the divine attributes.” Thus, he interpreted it according to the views of those who affirm the divine attributes, in opposition to Abū Hāmid’s view.

The hadith’s chain of transmission is not established, as the experts agree, and it is not related in the principal authoritative hadith collections. There is therefore no need to discuss its interpretation. Even supposing that the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—said it, its meaning is unspecified: it identifies no particular position. Hence, whenever someone claims that it refers to his own position, his opponent can make the same claim.

There is no doubt that the view of Yahyā ibn ‘Ammār, Abū Ismā‘īl al-Ansārī, and others who affirm the divine attributes is closer to the truth than that of those who deny them, since this knowledge is, by common agreement, part of the knowledge of the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—and of the Companions. It is well known that no one transmits the position of those who deny the attributes from the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—or his Companions, whether through an authentic or a weak chain. The position of those who affirm the attributes is quite otherwise: the Quran, the hadiths, and the reports from the Companions are full of it. How, then, can the Prophet’s words—Allah bless him and grant him peace—be understood as referring to knowledge that no one has transmitted from him, rather than to the knowledge transmitted from him and his Companions?

Likewise, al-Bukhārī related that ‘Alī—may Allah be pleased with him—said:

“Tell people what they can understand, and leave aside what they would reject. Would you like Allah and His Messenger to be disbelieved?”

Abū al-Walīd ibn Rushd the grandson, the philosopher, and others like him have interpreted this as referring to the teachings of the esotericists

---

## Page 6900

—the philosophers who deny the divine attributes. This is an obvious distortion, for ‘Alī’s words, “Would you like Allah and His Messenger to be disbelieved?” indicate that this concerns something the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—conveyed. Yet no Muslim has transmitted anything from the Prophet—Allah bless him and grant him peace—in support of the views of those who deny the attributes among the philosophers, Jahmiyyah, Qarāmitah, and Mu‘tazilah, through either an authentic or a weak chain. How, then, could Allah and His Messenger be disbelieved concerning something that no one has transmitted from Allah and His Messenger? By contrast, those who affirm the attributes have transmitted numerous, well-known hadiths describing the Lord’s attributes, His angels, His Paradise, and His Fire. These may be beyond some people’s understanding, so relating them may give rise to the fear that Allah and His Messenger will be disbelieved.

Of the same kind are the words of ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd:

“No man relates to people something beyond their understanding without its becoming a trial for some of them.”

In his statements, whether relating something or giving instructions, Ibn Mas‘ūd was among the most emphatic in affirming the attributes and among the most prolific transmitters of the hadiths concerning them. His students were among the most eminent of the Successors and the most emphatic in this regard. The same applies to the students of Ibn ‘Abbās. The more knowledgeable a Companion was, the more emphatically he and his students affirmed the attributes.

It is thus clear that the Companions did not inwardly conceal the opposite of what they outwardly professed. They did not outwardly affirm the attributes while inwardly denying them, nor outwardly affirm a command while inwardly holding its fulfilment to be impossible. Rather, they were the most steadfast in believing what the Messenger conveyed and obeying what he commanded. This is a broad subject, encompassing matters for which there is not enough space here

---

## Page 6901

to discuss them in detail. Yet we know the central principle: every utterance and deed necessarily has an outward and an inward aspect. The outward aspect of an utterance is what the tongue pronounces; its inward aspect is the realities and meanings present in the heart. The outward aspect of a deed is the movement of the body; its inward aspect is its underlying realities and the person’s intentions within the heart. Thus, when the hypocrite outwardly conformed to Islam without possessing the realities of faith, this did not benefit him, and he was among the losers. Indeed, he was in the lowest depths of the Fire. Allah Most High says:

“Among the people are some who say, ‘We believe in Allah and the Last Day,’ but they are not believers.”

“They seek to deceive Allah and those who believe, but they deceive only themselves, though they do not realize it.”

And the subsequent verses. At the beginning of Surah al-Baqarah, Allah revealed four verses describing the believers, two describing the disbelievers, and more than ten describing the hypocrites. Allah Most High also says:

“When the hypocrites come to you, they say, ‘We testify that you are indeed the Messenger of Allah.’ Allah knows that you are indeed His Messenger, and Allah testifies that the hypocrites are liars.”

And the rest of the surah. Allah Most High also says:

“Do not let those who hasten into disbelief grieve you—those who say with their mouths, ‘We believe,’ while their hearts do not believe.”

To the end of the verse.

The heretics outwardly profess agreement with the Muslims while inwardly concealing the opposite. They are worse than the hypocrites, for hypocrites are of two kinds: one kind outwardly professes faith while inwardly concealing disbelief, without claiming that the disbelief concealed within is the true reality of faith. The heretics, however, claim that the disbelief they conceal within is the true reality of faith

---

## Page 6902

and that the prophets and Allah’s allies are of their kind, concealing the same disbelief and theological negation that they themselves conceal. Thus, they combine concealing disbelief with claiming that this concealed belief is true faith according to those possessed of spiritual knowledge. They do not disclose to those who respond to them that what is concealed impugns the Messenger and the believers and declares him a liar. Rather, they present it as part of the Messenger’s perfection and the completeness of his spiritual state. They claim that what he did represents the utmost perfection, something only the most perfect of men can accomplish: governing people justly and developing the world along a virtuous path. Some groups may regard this as true both inwardly and outwardly. Their position thus ultimately makes hypocrisy the very reality of faith, although it is necessarily known that hypocrisy is the opposite of faith.

For this reason, their principal gateway has been Shiism and the Rāfidī doctrine, for the Rāfidīs are the most ignorant and mendacious of the sects, and the furthest removed from understanding transmitted sources and rational matters. They make dissimulation one of the foundations of their religion and attribute countless lies to the Prophet’s household—so many that only Allah can enumerate them. They even report that Jaʿfar al-Sādiq said, “Dissimulation is my religion and the religion of my forefathers.”

“Dissimulation” is the hallmark of hypocrisy, for its reality according to them is to say with their tongues what is not in their hearts—and this is the very reality of hypocrisy.

Since this is one of the foundations of their religion, whenever transmitters report anything from ʿAlī or another member of the Prophet’s household that agrees with Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah, they say, “They said this by way of dissimulation.” They then opened the door of hypocrisy to the Qarmatian esotericists

---

## Page 6903

—the philosophers among the Ismāʿīlīs, the Nusayrīs, and others like them. They treated the Messenger’s words as belonging to this same category: he outwardly expressed the opposite of what he inwardly concealed, and privately confided the opposite of what he publicly proclaimed. In effect, their position made the Messenger the leader of the hypocrites. Yet he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is the truthful and trustworthy one, who explains to people what has been sent down to them, conveys his Lord’s message, and addresses them in clear Arabic.

Allah Most High says:

“We have not sent any messenger except in the language of his people, so that he might make things clear to them.”

And He says:

“We have made it an Arabic Quran so that you may understand.”

And He says:

“We have certainly made the Quran easy for remembrance. Is there anyone who will take heed?”

And He says:

“We have made it easy in your language only so that you may give glad tidings through it to the righteous and warn a contentious people.”

And He says:

“The tongue of the one to whom they allude is foreign, whereas this is clear Arabic.”

And He says:

“We have sent down to you the Reminder so that you may explain to people what has been sent down to them.”

And He says:

“It is for Us to gather it and recite it.”

“So when We have recited it, follow its recitation.”

“Then it is for Us to explain it.”

And He says:

“This is a blessed Book which We have sent down to you, so that they may reflect upon its verses and those of understanding may take heed.”

And He says:

“Do they not reflect upon the Quran, or are there locks upon their hearts?”

And He says:

“Are the messengers responsible for anything but conveying the message clearly?”

And the messengers said:

“Our Lord knows that we have indeed been sent to you. Our duty is only to convey the message clearly.”

And He says:

“Say, ‘Obey Allah and obey the Messenger. But if you turn away, he is responsible only for what he has been charged with, and you are responsible for what you have been charged with. If you obey him, you will be guided. And the Messenger’s duty is only

---

## Page 6904

to convey the message clearly.’”

And Allah Most High says:

“Obey Allah and obey the Messenger. But if you turn away, Our Messenger’s duty is only to convey the message clearly.”

And He says:

“O Messenger, convey what has been sent down to you from your Lord. If you do not, then you have not conveyed His message.”

These and similar passages make clear that the messengers are required to convey the message clearly. The verbs bāna, abāna, istabāna, tabayyana, and bayyana can all be used intransitively to mean that something has become clear. One may also use abāna and bayyana to mean that someone else made it clear, and tabayyana and istabāna to mean that someone discerned it. It is well known that the messengers fulfilled their duty. Indeed, Allah took a covenant from the people of knowledge that they would explain that knowledge and not conceal it, and He condemned those who conceal it. Allah Most High says:

“And when Allah took a covenant from those who were given the Scripture: ‘You must make it clear to people and not conceal it.’”

And He says:

“Who is more unjust than one who conceals testimony he has received from Allah?”

And He says:

“Indeed, those who conceal the clear proofs and guidance We have sent down, after We have made them clear to people in the Scripture—Allah curses them, and so do those who curse.”

Thus, He cursed those who conceal it and declared that He had made it clear to people in the Scripture. How, then, could He have made it clear to people while concealing and hiding the truth, and outwardly expressing the opposite of what He kept inwardly? Had He remained silent instead of explaining the truth, He would have been concealing it. Anyone who accuses the prophets of lying and concealing the truth while still professing that they are prophets is among the worst and most wicked hypocrites, and among the most manifestly self-contradictory. And many people devoted to ascetic practice, worship, scholarship, and rational inquiry, who have followed the path of

---

## Page 6905

certain Sufis and spiritual mendicants and certain practitioners of speculative theology and philosophy, follow the approach of the esotericists in some matters, though not in all. Some even hold that certain members of the spiritual elite are exempt from prayer, that wine and other prohibited things are lawful for them, or that some of them have a path to Allah, Mighty and Majestic, other than following the Messenger.

Some invoke the story of Moses and al-Khidr, supposing that al-Khidr departed from the revealed law, and that other friends of Allah may therefore depart from it just as he did. They are misguided in this on two counts:

First, al-Khidr did not depart from the revealed law. Rather, what he did was permissible under the law of Moses. Thus, when he explained his reasons to Moses, Moses approved of his actions. Had they not been permissible, he would not have approved of them. But Moses did not know the reasons that made those actions permissible, so he thought that al-Khidr was like the unjust king. Al-Khidr then explained this to him.

Second, al-Khidr did not belong to Moses’ community, nor was he obliged to follow him. Rather, he said to him:

“I possess knowledge from Allah which Allah has taught me and which you do not know, and you possess knowledge from Allah which Allah has taught you and which I do not know.”
